# Spec: daemon-drain-before-stop

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| Status | in-progress |
| Scope | protocol |
| Depends | - |
| Phase | 3/6 (next: phase 4, BGP drain, waits for the in-flight IKE `RegisterProtocol` work to land; owner 2026-10-15) |
| Handoff | - |
| Updated | 2026-10-15 |

Recovery after compaction: `.claude/rules/post-compaction.md`.

## Task

Owner decision, 2026-10-13: "we need a drain phase for every protocol adding routes to the FIB/RIB" before the daemon stops.

Today a `ze` stop tears every routing protocol down at once: BGP sends Cease to every peer inside a 1 s budget, OSPF and IS-IS adjacencies drop when the plugin connection closes, and nothing tells the network to move traffic away first. Every neighbour loses its routes through Ze at the same instant and reconverges while traffic is black-holed. RFC 8326 (BGP graceful shutdown), RFC 6987 (OSPF MaxLinkMetric) and RFC 3787 (IS-IS overload) each describe how a router tells its neighbours to move traffic away before it leaves, and Ze performs none of them at stop.

Goal: a daemon-wide drain phase. After config changes are fenced and before any plugin is torn down, the daemon sends one `drain` RPC, in parallel, to every plugin that registered for it through the SDK. The RPC carries the stop kind (`stop` or `restart`), and each plugin decides what that kind means for its protocol. Each protocol makes itself unattractive (BGP GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN re-advertisement of the paths RFC 8326 Section 4.2 covers: every path sent over an eBGP session, and the originated and eBGP-learned paths sent to iBGP peers, those with LOCAL_PREF 0; OSPF max-metric; IS-IS overload), waits its configured time, and answers. The daemon bounds the phase with a ceiling counted from the start of the stop and kept below the supervisor's kill timeout, refuses a budget or a configured wait above that ceiling, and lets a signal received during the stop abort the drain (a later signal forces exit). On `restart`, BGP answers at once and takes the graceful-restart path (RFC 4724 Section 5).

`plan/immediate/spec-ospf-max-metric-startup-shutdown-windows.md` depends on this spec: its Q-1 (where the `on-shutdown` window runs) is answered by this spec's drain phase, and its interim refusal of `on-shutdown` above 0 (its AC-11) is replaced by AC-11 here. Q-6 deletes that leaf, `ospf max-metric router-lsa on-shutdown`, and replaces it with `ospf drain-on-shutdown`. That spec's rows naming the old leaf were repointed to the new one on 2026-10-13, citing this Q-6; its interim refusal keeps its meaning under the new name, and whichever of the two specs is implemented first performs the rename (Files to Modify, "OSPF rename").

## Owner Questions

The owner answered every row on 2026-10-13. The Answer column below holds each answer verbatim and is the durable record; `tmp/session/2026-10-13-9d2e6c21-74c1-4c42-b9ef-30145b95cfb1/scratch/owner-answers-2026-10-13.md` (rows "drain") is provenance only, since a session path is not retained (round 9, N-4). The design below is written under those answers; the options not chosen were deleted from the design with the answers (no dead alternative is kept).

| # | Question | Decided | Reason | Answer |
|---|----------|---------|----------------|--------|
| Q-1 | Daemon drain ceiling, and the systemd `TimeoutStopSec` it implies | (a) ceiling 60 s, teardown margin 30 s, `TimeoutStopSec=90`, fixed in code (no config leaf: the unit is generated at install time) | 90 s is systemd's default today, so an installed unit keeps its stop time; the teardown after the drain is about 12 s worst case (R-5), and the ceiling is counted from the start of the stop, so the config fence spends from it. The pinned gokrazy supervisor waits 100 s after SIGTERM before SIGKILL (A-5), which 60 s plus 30 s fits. The 100 s is gokrazy's default, which an update request can change through `wait_per_signal`; `ze push` sets no `wait_per_signal` (nothing under `internal/appliance` names it), so an upgrade reboot keeps the default, and this spec keeps it so (round 2, N-12). The arithmetic bounds the drain and the teardown; it does not bound an admitted commit reload still running in the fence, which can exceed the supervisor's timeout (Known Limitations, round 2 I-8). Design review round 1 (I-3, I-5) changed these numbers | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (60 s drain + 30 s teardown, stop timeout 90 s) |
| Q-2 | Operator drain for maintenance without stopping | runtime drain and its cancel, designed in its own spec: `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` | every vendor puts maintenance drain behind its own command with an undo and a status, and it needs an "undrain" RPC this spec does not otherwise need. The drain RPC here carries only what this spec reads (kind, restart cause, deadline); any field the runtime form needs is that spec's design (round 9, I-5) | 2026-10-13, owner: "we want runtime but it need design" (runtime drain/cancel, designed in its own spec) |
| Q-3 | Default of each protocol's drain wait | (a) 0 everywhere: the phase exists, and a stop is as fast as today until a wait is configured | a drain lengthens every stop, including in tests and labs; FRR, BIRD, GoBGP, Junos default to no stop-time drain (research §1.2) | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (default wait 0 everywhere) |
| Q-4 | RFC 8326 is `Enrolment: blocked` and the RFC scope was frozen on 2026-10-08 | (a) this owner decision lifts the freeze for the RFC 8326 sender side: this spec updates the ledger (summary `Support` row moves from `-`) and owes the extraction walk (`rfc/extraction/rfc8326.json`) | a sender implemented with `Support -` is a false public claim the other way. The receiver side (Section 4.1 inbound LOCAL_PREF on received GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN) stays out | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (lift the RFC freeze for the RFC 8326 sender side; ledger updated, extraction walk owed) |
| Q-5 | Deleting a protocol by commit (`delete ospf`, BGP removed at reload) | (a) stop only, as today | a commit has no ceiling, no second-signal abort and runs inside the transaction's apply budget | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (commit delete stops without drain) |
| Q-6 | Leaf names for the drain waits | one name under each protocol: `bgp drain-on-shutdown <seconds>`, `ospf drain-on-shutdown <seconds>`, `isis drain-on-shutdown <seconds>`. OSPF's `max-metric router-lsa on-shutdown` is deleted and replaced by `ospf drain-on-shutdown`, with no alias | an operator learns one name for one behaviour. Ze is pre-release, so the OSPF leaf is renamed with no compatibility shim (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`); the rename's YANG, parser, show, test and doc consequences are rows in Files to Modify | 2026-10-13, owner: "Why not the same name for all the protocols?" then "a"; the word: "drain-on-shutdown" |
| Q-7 | RSVP-TE (RFC 5817 TE graceful shutdown) and LDP | (a) no drain in this spec; LDP traffic moves with the IGP drain; RSVP-TE recorded as a gap, its implementation homed in `plan/spec-rsvp-te-graceful-shutdown-rfc5817.md` | RFC 5817 is not in `rfc/full/` and the RFC scope is frozen for it. LDP needs no drain of its own: LDP's labelled path follows the IGP's shortest path, so the OSPF max-metric or IS-IS overload moves LDP traffic whether or not LDP-IGP sync runs. RFC 5443 sync governs LDP bring-up (the IGP holds max cost until LDP is operational), not shutdown. OSPF implements it (`internal/plugins/ospf/ldp_sync.go`), IS-IS does not (A-7, corrected in design review round 1, I-8) | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (no RSVP-TE/LDP drain; RFC 5817 recorded as gap); note: "make a note to eventually implement RFC 5817 - not urgent" |
| Q-8 | Prefixes this router injects into OSPF/IS-IS (externals redistributed from static, connected, kernel, BGP; `default-information originate`; its own stub networks and IS-IS IP reachability) | (b) the drain also steers them, whenever a drain wait is set: OSPF externals (Type-5, Type-7) at metric 0xfffffe for type 2 and 0x7fffff for type 1, OSPF stub links and OSPFv3 Router-referencing prefixes at 0xffff (Cisco's `max-metric router-lsa external-lsa include-stub`); IS-IS IP reachability at 0x7f000000, half of MAX_PATH_METRIC (wide), and 63 (narrow). Each value is chosen so every receiver still routes on it for any realistic distance and prefers any realistic alternate, so the drain steers and never withdraws (round 9, B-1 and I-1: a type 1 external and a wide prefix are cut on distance plus value, so their maximum withdraws them) | RFC 6987 MaxLinkMetric moves transit only. RFC 2328 Section 11, Type 2 cost: "This cost has been advertised by an AS boundary router, and is the most significant part of the total path cost. For example, a type 2 external path with type 2 cost of 5 is always preferred over a path with type 2 cost of 10, regardless of the cost of the two paths' internal components." So a MaxLinkMetric Router-LSA does not move an E2 external off Ze, and RFC 6987 Section 2 keeps a stub link's cost. IS-IS overload keeps transit off the node, not the prefixes it advertises. Design: Data Flow "Injected prefixes" (Q-8), AC-31 to AC-34 | 2026-10-13, owner: "b" after asking "What would be the correct thing to do?" (also steer injected externals and stub prefixes: OSPF max metric on type-5/7 and stub, IS-IS highest usable metric <= MAX_PATH_METRIC, designed in this spec, on whenever a drain wait is set) |
| Q-9 | gokrazy appliance, including the `ze push` upgrade reboot | (a) the drain runs on the appliance the same way, and (u1) the `ze push` upgrade reboot is a plain stop: drain, then Cease | `ze push` talks only to the gokrazy updater: it streams the image, then calls `target.Reboot(ctx)` (`internal/appliance/cmd_push.go`, under `if !opts.noReboot`), and holds no SSH or API client to the running daemon. gokrazy's reboot handler sends the plain SIGTERM its supervisor sends to ze's whole process group, and waits 100 s before SIGKILL (A-5), which fits Q-1 (a). The upgrade reboot therefore drains and sends Cease, which costs the upgrade one reconvergence and needs no daemon verb, no client in `ze push` and no restart-arm state (design review rounds 1 to 3, I-2, I-4, I-5) | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" then "u1" (appliance drains as elsewhere; ze push upgrade is a plain stop: drain then Cease) |
| Q-10 | The obligation is RFC 8326 Section 4: "The LOCAL_PREF value SHOULD be lower than any of the alternative paths." Section 4.2's second bullet is the procedure the RFC gives for it: the initiator "applies an inbound BGP route policy on the EBGP session to be shutdown. This policy tags the paths received over the session with the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community and sets LOCAL_PREF to a low value." This design substitutes an egress LOCAL_PREF 0 on the copies Ze sends to iBGP peers and leaves Ze's own route selection unchanged. Is the Section 4 SHOULD met for Ze's own Loc-RIB? | (b) keep the egress substitution; the ledger row is the Section 4 SHOULD, recorded as met at every iBGP receiver (LOCAL_PREF 0 on every tagged iBGP copy) with the residue that Ze's own Loc-RIB keeps the path's LOCAL_PREF, as an owner-approved deviation of that SHOULD for the initiator's own selection, Section 4 cited, not counted conformant. Section 4.2 stays in the mapping table only as the procedure Ze substitutes | The Section 4.2 bullets describe an operator procedure and carry no RFC 2119 keyword, so they are not what a deviation is recorded against (round 2, I-2); the obligation-bearing sentence is Section 4's, which the egress LOCAL_PREF 0 meets at every iBGP peer. Lowering LOCAL_PREF in Ze's own decision process would move Ze's best path to an iBGP-learned alternate and so withdraw its own eBGP path from its iBGP peers, the outcome the RFC's "retain the paths" avoids. Ze's own transit traffic keeps the exit being shut down until the Cease, a residue the owner accepted. New in design review round 1 (I-11) | 2026-10-13, owner: "b" (LOCAL_PREF 0 on iBGP copies only; own selection unchanged, owner-approved deviation against RFC 8326 Section 4) |
| Q-11 | A destination whose per-peer `send-community` setting suppresses standard communities (code 8) cannot carry GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN. `forwardUpdateCore` and `forward_rs.go` apply `applyFactsSendCommunity` after their step loops (`applySendCommunityMask` suppresses code 8 through `mods.Op(8, filterapi.AttrModSuppress, nil)`), so on the forward rails the tag the drain step adds is removed again; the gate (`exportFilterForBody`) applies no send-community mask today, so there the tag would go out against the operator's setting. Does the drain honour the mask or override it? (Posed in round 4 against the per-rail design. Under the 2026-10-14 redesign f runs at the writer, after every rail's mask, so only the verdict's own reading of the mask, the answer (a), keeps the tag off a suppressing destination.) | (a) honour the mask: the verdict reads the destination's mask (`sendCommunitySuppression` over `PeerSettings.SendCommunity`, the one reader of the keywords) and, where code 8 is suppressed, drops the tag from its answer: toward eBGP the answer becomes untouched (a drain replay is withheld, RFC 4271 Section 9.2, and the writer counts the path withheld, not tagged; one log line per peer names the peer and the setting), toward iBGP it becomes LOCAL_PREF 0 without the community | the operator's configured `send-community` wins, as it does for every other community today, and the counter and log state what was actually sent. On the forward rails `applyFactsSendCommunity` already records a Suppress on code 8 in the same `mods`, and `genericCommunityHandler` drops the whole attribute when `filterapi.LastSetOrSuppress` finds a Suppress, so honouring the mask is what the existing plan already does there (round 6, N-5). RFC 8326 Section 4.2 describes a policy the operator applies, and the operator who disabled standard communities toward that peer has decided what it receives. The AC-9 cell and `TestBGPDrainSendCommunityMask` are written under (a). New in design review round 4 (I-5) | 2026-10-13, owner: "a" (respect send-community suppression; eBGP replay withheld, iBGP LOCAL_PREF 0 without tag, one log line per peer) |

## Required Reading

### Architecture Docs
- [ ] `docs/architecture/plugin/plugin-system.md` - registration, engine boundary, `OnStarted` vs `OnAllPluginsReady`
  → Constraint: a new core-to-plugin call is an RPC on the plugin connection (DirectBridge for in-process), never an EventBus event; the drain is a request with a reply
  → Constraint: Stage 1 declarations are pure values; a `DrainBudget` declared at Stage 1 is validated in `startup.go` before it is stored, the way `validateDoctorCheckDecls` (`internal/component/plugin/server/startup.go`) refuses a malformed doctor-check declaration. It is NOT validated like `VerifyBudget`/`ApplyBudget`: `startup.go` copies those into the registration unchecked, and `capBudget` (`internal/component/config/transaction/orchestrator_budget.go`) clamps them to `MaxBudgetSeconds` (600) later, silently. A drain budget over the ceiling is refused, never clamped (registration structs: `internal/component/plugin/registration.go`, `pkg/plugin/rpc/types.go`)
- [ ] `pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_dispatch.go` `eventLoop` (socket) and `bridgeEventLoop` (DirectBridge) - the SDK runs every engine-to-plugin callback, including execute-command, one at a time on one loop
  → Constraint: an `OnDrain` handler that waits its configured time inside the callback freezes every command and RPC the plugin answers for that whole time, so `show bgp` (the BGP summary) over SSH would hang during the drain. The SDK MUST run the drain handler off the event loop: the loop hands the drain request, over a channel, to one long-lived drain worker per connection and goes back to serving callbacks, and the worker answers the drain request's ID when the handler returns. That an answer may follow later requests on the same connection is A-11 (design review round 1, B-2). The worker is started once per connection when the plugin has registered `OnDrain`, reads that channel, serves its requests in arrival order, and returns when the connection closes: "Every goroutine MUST be a long-lived worker reading a channel: a goroutine started inside an event loop or per message is forbidden" (`ai/rules/goroutine-lifecycle.md`). It holds one of two states, `running` and `stopping`: a `drain` of kind `stop` or `restart` moves it to `stopping`, and in `stopping` a second stop-kind `drain` is refused with an error naming the plugin, whether the first is still running or has answered (round 3, N-9; AC-28). The worker cannot make that refusal while the first stop runs, because it serves in arrival order and reads the second request only after the first handler returns, up to `drain-on-shutdown` later. The state is therefore one atomic per connection, shared by the event loop and the worker, and the event loop reads and writes it: when the event loop queues a stop-kind `drain` it moves the state to `stopping`, and a stop-kind `drain` that arrives in `stopping` is refused by the event loop before it is queued, naming the plugin. The worker reads the state and never moves it out of `stopping` (runtime review r2, I-3). The worker, rather than a one-time goroutine, is built from the start because `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` adds re-enterable requests to it, so nothing here is written to be replaced (cross-spec amendment, 2026-10-14; `ai/rules/no-layering.md`)
- [ ] `docs/architecture/api/process-protocol.md` - wire protocol, `bye`, shutdown
  → Constraint: `ProcessManager.Stop` sends no `bye` today; closing the connection IS the shutdown signal. The drain RPC must therefore run before `apiServer.Stop()`, while connections are open
- [ ] `docs/architecture/behavior/signals.md` - signal page
  → Constraint: the page describes ExaBGP's Python handler (SIGHUP = shutdown, SIGALRM restart); Ze reloads on SIGHUP. It disagrees with the code today and this spec rewrites it (Doc row 12)
- [ ] `docs/architecture/hub-architecture.md` - hub stop orchestration
  → Decision: the drain step lives in the hub's `run` stop path between `commitReloads.close()` and `gnmiSrv.Stop()`, so management (SSH `show`) stays up while draining and config is already fenced. Management answering also needs the SDK constraint above: a plugin whose drain blocked its event loop could not answer its own `show` commands (AC-23)
- [ ] `ai/rules/plugins.md`, `ai/rules/goroutine-lifecycle.md`, `ai/rules/architecture.md`
  → Constraint: the daemon must not name BGP/OSPF/IS-IS: plugins opt in by registering an `OnDrain` handler and a `DrainBudget`; the hub fans out over whoever registered
  → Constraint: the parallel fan-out starts no goroutine per stop. `CallRPC` blocks until the answer or its context ends (`pkg/plugin/rpc/mux.go`, `conn.go`, `bridge.go` `SendCallback`, `internal/component/plugin/ipc/rpc.go`, read 2026-10-14), so the calls overlap through long-lived workers reading a channel: one server drain caller per drain-registered plugin generation, started with the generation and returning when it ends (Data Flow step 4)
  → Constraint: a mux call whose context ends deletes its pending entry, so the answer is dropped (`MuxConn.CallRPC`); the caller therefore calls under the generation's context, never under the drain deadline, and the deadline bounds only how long the fan-out reads replies
- [ ] `docs/architecture/api/architecture.md` "Adj-RIB-Out (Plugin Owned)" and the `exportFilterForBody` passage; `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md`; `docs/architecture/bgp/structural-forwarding.md`
  → Constraint: "the engine holds no Adj-RIB-Out. It stores no route at all." The Adj-RIB-Out with attributes belongs to bgp-rib, so the drain replay is bgp-rib's (round 2, B-1)
  → Constraint: rib-storage-design: "Address equality or an absent source field is not proof of origin." A replay write that declares no provenance is therefore never classed as an origination by the drain verdict (round 2, B-2)
- [ ] `docs/guide/operations.md` - operator stop description (the signal table row "Graceful shutdown (NOTIFICATION Cease to all peers)")
  → Constraint: must describe the drain phase, the signal state table, and the `restart` kind
- [ ] `docs/architecture/appliance/ota-push.md` - `ze push` upgrade and reboot through the gokrazy updater
  → Constraint: the upgrade reboot reaches ze as gokrazy's plain SIGTERM (Q-9 (u1)): the page states that an upgrade reboot drains, then sends Cease

### RFC Summaries (Scope: protocol)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc8326.md` (full text `rfc/full/rfc8326.txt`) - BGP graceful shutdown
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4.2: "In the case of a shutdown of the whole router, in addition to the graceful shutdown of all EBGP sessions, there is a need to gracefully shut down the routes originated by this router (e.g., BGP aggregates redistributed from other protocols, including static routes). This can be performed by tagging these routes with the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community and setting LOCAL_PREF to a low value."
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4: "The goal of this procedure is to retain the paths to be shut down between the peers, but with a lower LOCAL_PREF value, allowing the paths to remain in use while alternate paths are selected and propagated, rather than simply withdrawing the paths." Section 4.2, first bullet: the outbound policy "tags the paths propagated over the session with the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community". The procedure covers the EBGP sessions being shut down and the routes the router originates; it says nothing that covers an iBGP-learned path reflected to an iBGP client. The untagged branches of the drain verdict (reflected paths, learned paths sent to a route-server client) rest on this scope, not on RFC 4456 (round 2, I-3). The RFC states the scope directly. Section 1: "Other maintenance cases, such as the shutdown of an IBGP session or the establishment of an EBGP session, are out of scope for this document." Appendix C.1 (informative): "For the shutdown of an IBGP session, provided the IBGP topology is viable after the maintenance of the session (i.e., if all BGP speakers of the AS have an IBGP signaling path for all prefixes advertised on this graceful shutdown IBGP session), then the shutdown of an IBGP session does not lead to transient unreachability. As a consequence, no specific graceful shutdown action is required." Both are quoted at the reflected branch (round 3, N-5)
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4.3: "BGP Implementers SHOULD provide configuration knobs that utilize the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community to inform BGP neighbors in preparation for an impending neighbor shutdown." The whole-router stop drain (`drain-on-shutdown`) is such a knob for the whole router; a per-neighbour knob is not in this spec (Q-2), so the Section 4.3 row is partial (round 3, I-7)
  → Constraint: the first bullet tags "the paths propagated over the session", every one of them, with no condition on the next hop: a path Ze sends to an eBGP peer with its next hop unchanged (third-party next hop) is tagged too, because the receiver loses it at the Cease whatever its next hop (round 2, I-1, rejected half)
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4.1, the receiver procedure: "an inbound BGP route policy is applied on all EBGP sessions of the ASBR." The community alone moves no iBGP receiver, which is why every tagged iBGP copy also carries LOCAL_PREF 0
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4.2, second bullet: the initiator "applies an inbound BGP route policy on the EBGP session to be shutdown. This policy tags the paths received over the session with the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community and sets LOCAL_PREF to a low value." This design substitutes an egress LOCAL_PREF 0 toward iBGP peers for it (Q-10, mapping in RFC Documentation)
  → Constraint: RFC 8326 Section 4: "The RECOMMENDED value is 0." (LOCAL_PREF); Section 4.2 gives no timer, only "waits for route re-advertisement over the EBGP session and for BGP routing convergence on both ASBRs", then "shuts down the EBGP session, optionally using [RFC8203]"
  → Constraint: summary Meta today: `Enrolment: blocked`, `Support: -`; this spec moves both and owes the extraction walk (Q-4 (a), RFC Documentation)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc4456.md` (full text `rfc/full/rfc4456.txt`) - route reflection
  → Constraint: RFC 4456 Section 10: "In addition, when a RR reflects a route, it SHOULD NOT modify the following path attributes: NEXT_HOP, AS_PATH, LOCAL_PREF, and MED." The list holds LOCAL_PREF and not COMMUNITIES, so Section 10 is cited only on the verdict's LOCAL_PREF branch: a reflected path keeps its LOCAL_PREF. It is not the reason a reflected path stays untagged (that is RFC 8326 scope, above). It is the reason a reflected path whose next hop Ze rewrote to itself (`next-hop self` on a reflector) stays untagged too: tagging it would need LOCAL_PREF 0, which Section 10 says a reflector SHOULD NOT set, and the community alone moves no iBGP receiver (Section 4.1 above) (round 2, I-1 and I-3)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc7947.md` (full text `rfc/full/rfc7947.txt`) - route server
  → Constraint: RFC 7947 Section 2.2.4: "Transitive as well as non-transitive Communities attributes applied to an NLRI UPDATE sent to a route server SHOULD NOT be modified, processed, or removed, except as defined by local policy." The sentence covers paths a client sent to the route server, so a learned path Ze sends to a route-server client stays untagged; a path Ze originates was never sent to a route server, and is tagged toward a route-server client like any eBGP destination (round 2, I-3). Section 2.2 ("Optional recognized and unrecognized BGP attributes ... SHOULD NOT be updated by the route server") says the same for every optional attribute
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc4271.md` - BGP-4 Adj-RIB-Out
  → Constraint: RFC 4271 Section 9.2: "A BGP speaker SHOULD NOT advertise a given feasible BGP route from its Adj-RIB-Out if it would produce an UPDATE message containing the same BGP route as was previously advertised." The drain replay re-sends a path only when the drain verdict changes it; a path the verdict leaves untouched is withheld from the drain replay (Data Flow 5). A ROUTE-REFRESH answered during the drain is a refresh, which re-sends everything as today (RFC 2918 Section 4, quoted at bgp-rib `sendRoutes`)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc6987.md` (full text read) - OSPF stub router
  → Constraint: RFC 6987 Section 2: "router X announces its router-LSA to the neighbors with the cost of all non-stub links (links of the types other than 3) being set to MaxLinkMetric"; Section 3 defines MaxLinkMetric 0xffff. No shutdown timer in the RFC. Section 2: "The solution above applies to both OSPFv2 [RFC2328] and OSPFv3 [RFC5340]", so the drain covers OSPFv3 too (AC-11); the OSPFv3-only R-bit of Section 2.1 is not used (I-9)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc3787.md` - IS-IS overload bit
  → Constraint: the overload bit in LSP number zero keeps transit off the node (already quoted in the `overload` leaf of `ze-isis-conf.yang`)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc4724.md` - graceful restart
  → Constraint: a NOTIFICATION forecloses retention (Section 5); restart/reboot stop BGP in silence (`Reactor.StopForRestart`), so BGP's drain handler on stop kind `restart` re-advertises nothing and sends no Cease. The restart-kind reason is BGP's alone: the drain RPC still reaches the IGP plugins, which decide for their own protocol (I-2)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc3623.md` (full text `rfc/full/rfc3623.txt`) - OSPF graceful restart, restarter side on kind `restart`
  → Constraint: RFC 3623 Section 2.1: "If Router X wants to ensure that its neighbors receive the grace-LSAs, it should retransmit the grace-LSAs until they are acknowledged (i.e., perform standard OSPF reliable flooding of the grace-LSAs). If one or more fully adjacent neighbors do not receive grace-LSAs, they will more than likely cause premature termination of the graceful restart procedure (see Section 4)." The drain reply therefore waits for the acknowledgements or the deadline (round 4, I-7)
  → Constraint: RFC 3623 Appendix A: "Encodes the reason for the router restart as one of the following: 0 (unknown), 1 (software restart), 2 (software reload/upgrade) or 3 (switch to redundant control processor)." Section 3.1 gives as example helper policies "a) never act as helper, b) never allow the grace period to exceed a Time T, c) only help on software reloads/upgrades, or d) never act as a helper for specific routers (specified by OSPF Router ID)", so the reason is chosen per entry point (round 4, I-8)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc5443.md` - LDP-IGP sync
  → Constraint: sync governs bring-up: the IGP advertises maximum cost on a link until LDP is operational on it. It plays no part at shutdown: LDP traffic follows the IGP path, so the IGP drain moves it with or without sync. OSPF implements sync (`internal/plugins/ospf/ldp_sync.go`, `ldpSyncManager`); IS-IS does not (A-7, I-8)
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc9568.md` - VRRP v3
  → Constraint: RFC 9568 Section 6.4.3, Active state: "If a Shutdown event is received, then: ... Send an ADVERTISEMENT with Priority = 0". Ze already does this at teardown (A-9); VRRP needs no drain
- [ ] `rfc/short/rfc8203.md` / `rfc/short/rfc9003.md` - shutdown communication
  → Decision: the post-drain Cease keeps today's subcode 2 Administrative Shutdown

**Key insights:**
- There is no point today where all plugins are connected and the daemon waits for protocol work: every stop budget is 3 s or less, BGP's Cease budget is 1 s.
- `request shutdown` and SSH `stop` stop the BGP reactor (Cease) BEFORE the hub's ordered teardown starts, so a drain in the hub alone would run after BGP already left. Both paths must route through the hub.
- `request shutdown` is not the only handler that calls `r.Stop()` first: `handleDaemonQuit` does too. `stopDaemonForPlugin` (plugin failure policy) already goes through `shutdownFunc`, so it reaches the hub (I-6).
- Restart/reboot already stopped the reactor silently before the hub runs; the hub needs to know the stop kind and passes it in the drain RPC.
- Three supervisors deliver the stop SIGTERM to more than ze: systemd's default `KillMode=control-group` signals every process in the cgroup, the gokrazy supervisor signals ze's whole process group (`syscall.Kill(-pid)`, `service.Signal` in the pinned module), and a terminal Ctrl+C signals the foreground process group. External plugins are spawned by `exec.CommandContext` through `sh -c` with no `Setpgid`, so they share ze's process group, and they stop on SIGTERM through `sdk.SignalContext`: every one of them dies at the start of the drain. The fix is two parts: external plugins get their own process group, and the unit gets `KillMode=mixed` (the cgroup is not a process group) (I-3).
- The route-source registry is `redistevents.RegisterProtocol`; MPLS label sources (LDP, RSVP-TE) and VRRP addresses are outside it, and so are several direct kernel-route installers (table below).
- The BGP egress rails are more than two, and the drain tag must reach all of them by construction, not by inspection (B-3, Current Behavior). They run two different egress chains, `r.orderedEgressSteps` (`forwardUpdateCore` and every rail built on it) and `r.egressFilters` (the route server only) (round 3, B-1), but every rail ends at one writer, `Session.writeUpdateBody`, so the drain is applied there once and sits in neither chain (redesign 2026-10-14).
- The drain walk and the writes in flight reach bgp-rib on different paths, so their order is made by a barrier, not assumed (round 3, B-2).
- The tag decision needs to know where each path came from (originated, learned from eBGP, learned from iBGP), and the rails do not all carry that today: the route-server rail's source has no LocalAS, the LLGR stale rail passes a zero source, and the originated-route gate `exportFilterForBody` receives no source at all. Each writer's provenance is named in Data Flow 5 (round 2, B-2; redesign 2026-10-14), read at the writer: for a forward, the owner path the existing `fwdItem.writePath` yields (`fwdItem.receivedPeer`, or the section span's source) classed by `PeerSettings.IsIBGP`, with no new field (redesign round 1, I-2); for a gated write, from facts the reactor already holds under `writeMu`: the writer's owner item `Session.sentForward` and the destination's adj-out owner of the body's NLRI (round 3, N-1, I-1; round 4, I-11, which removed the provenance channel from bgp-rib).
- The reactor's Adj-RIB-Out keeps ownership and signatures, not attributes, so it cannot be replayed. The only per-peer copy of what was sent, with attributes and provenance, is bgp-rib's `ribOut` (round 2, B-1).

## Current Behavior (MANDATORY)

**Source files read:**
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main.go` `run`, the code after `waitLoop` returns - arms `forceExitOnSignal` on its own channel (`forceCh`, SIGINT/SIGTERM), `close(reloadCh)`, `awaitReloadWorker(reloadShutdownGrace)`, `stopConfirmWindow`, `commitReloads.close()`, prints "Shutting down (Ctrl+C again to force)...", `gnmiSrv.Stop()`, `apiShutdowns` (3 s ctx), `apiServer.Stop()`, `eng.Stop(3 s)`, reboot if `rebootRequested`. Verified.
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main.go` `forceExitOnSignal` - the next SIGINT/SIGTERM prints "forced exit" and `os.Exit(1)`. Verified. `forceCh` is armed BEFORE `close(reloadCh)`, so a signal during the config fence force-exits today. `forceCh` hears only OS signals: a `request shutdown` ends `waitLoop` through an injected value on `stopCh`, so after it the operator's FIRST OS signal is the one `forceExitOnSignal` reads (I-4).
  → Decision: e88979df59 replaced it in the daemon with the phase table `stopSignals` (`cmd/ze/hub/drain.go`, `TestHubSignalStateTable`); 83e496cba0 moved the helper into `cmd/ze/hub/service_web.go`, beside its only caller, the standalone `ze web` (owner, 2026-10-14: "Accept the move"), rather than wire it back into the daemon, where a second reader of the stop signals would force-exit during the drain.
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main_reload.go` `awaitReloadWorker`, `reloadShutdownGrace` - waits the reload worker 3 s, then cancels it and waits `<-done` with no bound; `reloadGate.close()` then waits admitted commit reloads (`wg.Wait()`) with no bound. The fence before the drain therefore has no upper bound today (I-5).
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main.go` teardown contexts - `apiShutdowns` share one 3 s context; `stopCtx` (3 s) is created BEFORE `apiServer.Stop()`, which itself spends up to `txShutdownGrace` 3 s plus `pluginStopGrace` 3 s, so `eng.Stop(stopCtx)` receives what is left of 3 s, often nothing (I-5).
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main.go` `apiServer.SetShutdownFunc` closure - injects SIGTERM into `stopCh`.
- [ ] `cmd/ze/hub/main_reload.go` `reloadGate` - `close()` refuses later commit reloads with `errDaemonStopping` ("daemon is shutting down; config reload refused") and waits admitted ones. This is the config fence the drain sits behind.
- [ ] `internal/component/plugin/server/system.go` `handleDaemonShutdown` - with a real BGP reactor: `r.Stop()` (Cease) then `SignalShutdownRequested`; reactorless: `shutdownFunc` (SIGTERM to stopCh). `handleDaemonReboot`: `rebootFunc` then signal. `handleDaemonQuit`: dumps every goroutine stack, then the same shape as shutdown: with a real reactor `r.Stop()` (Cease) then `SignalShutdownRequested`, reactorless `shutdownFunc` (I-6).

Its only caller is `applyFailurePolicy`, which runs when the plugin process has exited ("plugin exited"), so the failed plugin has no live connection by the time the drain fans out (round 2, N-11).

It skips events carrying the `replay` or `rib-lifecycle` meta, so a replay does not overwrite the entry it re-sent. `peerMeta[addr]` holds each peer's `PeerASN`/`LocalASN` (`rib_structured.go`) (round 2, B-1).

Each `replayGroup` carries one `LocalOrigin`/`SourcePeer`. These commands enter the reactor on the injection rail, through `exportFilterForBody` (round 2, B-1, B-2).
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/adj_rib_out.go` `adjRIBOut`, `adjRIBOutRoute` - "adjRIBOutRoute retains no forwarded attributes"; "Its only readers are final writers under writeMu ... There is no independently readable committed cache". Each entry keeps its owner (`adjOutPath.source`, nil for a local advertisement) and, for local routes, the signature of the body sent. It cannot be replayed (round 2, B-1; the round-1 reading that it could was wrong).
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/session_ownership.go` `adjOutWrite.allow` - suppression runs only for a write that is not raw and not source-bound, over an entry whose `owner.source == nil` (a local route), and only when `!replay && sig.equal(entry.signature)`. A body that gained a community has a different signature and is never suppressed, and the `replay` meta already bypasses suppression. The round-1 premise that the drain re-advertisement would be swallowed was wrong (round 2, B-1). Per authority (round 4, I-11): for `adjOutExpected` (a receipt) `allow` refuses a key with no entry or a different revision and otherwise sets `owner = entry.owner`, so the stored owner, not the command, is the provenance; `adjOutForwarded` refuses an owner whose `source` is nil with `errAdjOutProvenance`; `adjOutInitial` refuses an owner whose `source` is not `item.receivedPeer`. `adjOutPath.source` (`adj_rib_out.go`) is a `*Peer`, nil only for a local advertisement, and the `*Peer` keeps its settings while its session is down. `fwdItem` (`forward_pool.go`) already carries `initialLocal` and `receivedPeer` for a peer-up replay. The `replay` meta is a bool to the reactor (`replayFromMeta` in `update_text.go`, and two `sentMeta["replay"].(bool)` reads in `session_ownership.go`) and a presence test to bgp-rib (`handleSent`, `rib_structured.go`). The bool also travels as a parameter through `peer_send.go` (`sendUpdateWithSplit`, `sendUpdateOwnedWithSplit`), `peer.go` (`QueueAnnounce(..., replay bool)`, `peerOp.Replay`) and `peer_initial_sync.go` (the `op.Replay` reads); only `ls_export`'s receipt-less `replay: true` refresh uses the opQueue carrier, because a receipt batch never enters the opQueue (`reactor_api_batch.go`, `queued = false`) (round 5, N-3). Keying (round 5, I-1): `allow` keys each NLRI through `adjOutWrite.key`, which takes `addPath` from the destination encoding (`w.encoding.AddPathFor(fam)`, `encoding` = `bgpctx.Registry.Get(s.sendCtxID)`), strips the 4-byte path ID and passes it to `fwdPathKeyFor` separately, and the NLRI are split per section (IPv4 body, MP_REACH) with `nlrisplit.Get(fam)` (`adjOutWrite.section`, `filter`); for `adjOutExpected`, `allow` refuses a key with no entry and a key whose `entry.revision` differs from the owner revision `item.writePath` yields, and only then sets `owner = entry.owner`.
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_api_relay.go` `RelayStoredRoute` - the bgp-adj-rib-in stored-route relay to one destination; "Each route names the peer it was learned from", and the relay reaches `forwardUpdateCore` with that source.
- [ ] BGP egress rails (B-3; corrected in round 3, B-1). The reactor builds TWO egress chains, side by side in `reactor.go`: `r.egressFilters = filterapi.EgressFilters()` and `r.orderedEgressSteps = buildOrderedEgressSteps()`. Only the route-server forward `reactorForwardRS` / `reactorForwardRSSection` (`forward_rs.go`, `for _, filter := range r.egressFilters`) iterates `r.egressFilters`. `forwardUpdateCore` (`reactor_api_forward.go`, `for i := range a.r.orderedEgressSteps`) iterates the second chain, whose field doc says "orderedEgressSteps is the egress twin, used by forwardUpdateCore only": `buildOrderedEgressSteps` (`filter_ordered.go`) merge-sorts `filterapi.EgressOrdered()` with one reactor-bound step, the export policy chain (`policyChain: true`, stage `filterapi.FilterStagePeerChain` = 300, which sorts last), and its comment says "The RS fast path and injected-route path keep consuming r.egressFilters directly and are not built here". Every other forward rail is built on `forwardUpdateCore`: `ForwardUpdatesDirect` (`reactor_api_forward_batch.go`), `RelayStoredRoute` (`reactor_api_relay.go`), and the departure-replacement write `queueRecovery` (`relay_recovery.go`). In-process steps defer into `mods`, the policy step returns a wire override, and `buildModifiedPayload` applies `mods` on top of the override, so a step's place relative to the policy decides only whether a policy reject short-circuits it. `forwardUpdateCore` and the relay rails fill the source `filterInfo` only when `len(r.egressFilters) > 0`. Originated and injected routes are gated in `Session.writeUpdateGated` (`session_write.go`, `gate == true`) by `exportFilterForBody` (`egress_inject_filter.go`), which runs the PEER's configured export policy (`facts.exportFilters`) and next-hop normalization, and does NOT run `r.egressFilters`. The LLGR stale readvertise runs the `Readvertise`-opted subset only. The final writers are several (`writeUpdateGated`, `writeOrdinaryUpdateBody`, `writeRawUpdateBody`, `writeUpdateBody`), some writing a pre-encoded raw body, so "the final write point" is not one place either. `Reactor.Stop`'s comment in `reactor.go` records why a rail list kept by inspection fails: "the rails are found by inspection and the list is never finished".
- [ ] Provenance per rail (round 2, B-2). `forwardUpdateCore` builds the source `PeerFilterInfo` with PeerAS and LocalAS ("Kept in sync so no egress filter ever reads a silent zero from src.LocalAS", `reactor_api_forward.go`). `reactorForwardRS`'s `srcFilter` (`forward_rs.go`) sets Address, PeerAS, Name and GroupName, and no LocalAS: an egress filter reads a silent zero there today. `decideStaleReadvertise` (`reactor_api_batch.go`) hands every Readvertise filter a zero source (`var src filterapi.PeerFilterInfo`); the stale batch is then written by `sendBatchUpdate` through the gated writer, so a filter both Readvertise-registered and called from the gate would run twice on it. `exportFilterForBody(peer, body)` receives no source and no meta, and returns at once, with no policy and no next-hop normalization, when `peer.forwardFacts()` is nil ("No established forwarding snapshot: retain the existing admission"). It is reached through the session closure `egressRouteFilter` (set in `peer_run.go`, typed in `session.go`), whose two callers are `writeUpdateGated` and `Session.SendAnnounce`. Under `writeMu` the write's owner item is `Session.sentForward` (`sendUpdateCounted`). That item is nil only on the `sendUpdateWithSplit` paths: config-static initial sync (`peer_initial_sync.go`), static wire (`peer_static_wire.go`), the withheld re-send in `reactor_api_batch.go`, and `SendAnnounce`. Every plugin or API write through `AnnounceNLRIBatch` carries an owner, which `sendBatchUpdate` builds as `fwdItem{peer, session, originated: true}` and gives authority `adjOutExpected` for a receipt or `adjOutInitial` for a peer-up replay; with neither, the authority stays `adjOutUnspecified`, and `beginAdjOut` refuses only `adjOutUnspecified && !item.originated` (`session_ownership.go`), so an originated item with unspecified authority is the ordinary origination (redistribute, watchdog, ls_export, operator `update text`) (round 3, I-1). Source iBGP-ness is `PeerSettings.IsIBGP`, which is `isIBGPWith(PeerAS)` (`session_as_migration.go`): true when the peer AS equals `LocalAS` or the configured `MigrationAS` (RFC 7705), so comparing `PeerFilterInfo.PeerAS` with `LocalAS` disagrees with it under AS migration, and `forward_rs.go`'s `srcFilter` leaves `PeerAS` zero when `findPeerByAddr` fails (round 3, N-1). bgp-rs's `update text` and `update hex` writes are withdrawals and End-of-RIB only (`sendBatchedWithdrawals` and the EOR in `rs/server_handlers.go`), so they carry no path to tag. The closure built in `peer_run.go` calls `p.reactor.exportFilterForBody(p, body)` and is assigned to `session.egressRouteFilter`, so its construction already has the session in scope; `writeUpdateGated` calls it inside the `writeMu` section in which `sendUpdateCounted` (or the forward worker) set `sentForward` and `sentMeta` (round 4, I-11). `Peer.SendAnnounce` (`peer_send.go`) has no non-test caller: its only call site is its own call to `Session.SendAnnounce` (round 4, N-4). Send-community: `forwardUpdateCore` (`reactor_api_forward.go`) and the route-server rail (`forward_rs.go`) call `applyFactsSendCommunity` after their filter loops, which suppresses code 8 for a destination whose standard communities are disabled (`applySendCommunityMask`, `peer_forward_facts.go`); `exportFilterForBody` applies no send-community mask (round 4, I-5, Q-11).
- [ ] Writers of a forwarded body (round 4, I-2, I-3). Two writers take a destination's `writeMu` for a pre-filtered forward: the forward worker's batch handler (`forward_pool.go`: `session.sentMeta = item.meta`, `session.sentForward = item`, then `writeRawUpdateBody` for `item.rawBodies` and `writeUpdateGated(update, item.originated)` for `item.updates`, "originated items retain the ordinary writer's export gate"), and the route-server direct path `tryDirectWriteNoFlush` (`forward_rs.go`: `session.writeMu.TryLock()`, then the same `sentMeta`/`sentForward` assignments, then inline writes), whose verdict is computed before it takes `writeMu`. `fwdItem.meta` is the received UPDATE's meta map, shared by every destination's item (`forwardUpdateCore` and `reactorForwardRS` build `fwdItem{peer: peer, meta: update.Meta, ...}` per destination), so no writer may add a key to it. `reactor_notify.go` already shows the per-write shape: where the sent event is built it copies `meta` into a fresh map before adding `config-static` (`maps.Copy(merged, sentMeta)`). That site cannot read a session field: `notifyMessageReceiver` receives the meta as an argument and its `config-static` merge reads a Peer atomic (`peer.sendingConfigStatic.Load()`), and its comment records that an unlocked read of `peer.session` there "raced the peer run goroutine ... reading a reconnected session's writeMu-guarded field without its writeMu". The sent event of every forwarded body, raw or not, is raised at the `s.onMessageReceived(..., s.sentMeta, sourcePeer, s.sentSourceMessageID)` call in `Session.writeUpdateBody` (`session_write.go`), inside `writeMu` (round 5, I-2). The forward worker does not clear `sentMeta` per write: it overwrites it per item (`session.sentMeta = item.meta`) and clears it only in the batch-end defer (round 5, I-3). `fwdBucketMerge` (`forward_bucket.go`) rebuilds each merged item as a new `fwdItem` from a fixed field list (peer, raw bodies, meta, source peer string, source message ID, received peer and generation), and its run condition compares the same fields (round 5, N-2). An originated item queued behind forwards (`queueBehindForwards`, `fwdItem{..., originated: true}`) is written through the gated writer, `writeUpdateGated(update, true)`; under this design every item, forwarded or originated, is judged at write time in `writeUpdateBody` under `writeMu` (redesign 2026-10-14).

Egress filters are already registered by gr, filter_community, rs, role and `reactor/filter`, so every build already runs a chain per destination on every forward rail: `r.egressFilters` on the route-server rail, `r.orderedEgressSteps` (never empty: it always holds the policy chain step) on `forwardUpdateCore` (round 2, I-9; round 3, B-1). Stage constants are declared once in `filterapi.go` (`FilterStageProtocol` 0, `FilterStagePolicy` 100, `FilterStageAnnotation` 200, `FilterStagePeerChain` 300), "Gaps between values allow inserting new stages without renumbering".

An inter-plugin command is a different path from `eventChan`, so without such a barrier a sent event can still be queued when the command runs (round 3, B-2).
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_pool_barrier.go` `fwdPool.Barrier` - a sentinel per worker, "reaching the sentinel guarantees all prior items have been written to the wire"; exposed as `FlushForwardPool` (`reactor_api.go`) (round 3, B-2).

These are called from `DispatchNLRIGroups`, which both `update text` and `update hex` reach: `handleUpdateWire` (`update_wire.go`) ends in `DispatchNLRIGroups(ctx, result.Groups)`, and most `ribOut` replays are `update hex`, because `FormatAnnounceCommand` emits `update hex attr set` whenever `RawAttrs` is set (A-17 confirmed, round 4, N-1). `reactor_api_batch.go` `sendBatchUpdate` diverts a receipt write for a peer with forwards owed to `queueBehindForwards(batch.SentOwnerMessage, batch.Replay)`, which rebuilds the `fwdItem` (`forward_pool.go`) and copies only `expectedMessage` and `replay` (`item.meta = {"replay": true}`) (round 3, I-2).

No `.ci` has an OSPF or IS-IS wire peer: the only wire expectation across `test/` is `expect=bgp`, and the other OSPF `.ci` files wrap `go test` (round 4, I-6). That shape ends with the observer's own `request shutdown` (`observeConfigured` in the fixture), so it cannot express a SIGTERM stop, which needs `cmd=background` plus `action=sigterm` (`test/reload/signal-quit.ci`); and `ospf-ldp-sync-show.ci` records that "the ospf suite does not provision the hub TLS acceptor natively", so it runs in the full CI / QEMU environment and carries `skip-os` darwin (round 5, N-7). No `.ci` drives SSH exec `restart` against a running daemon, and `ze-system-cmd.yang` `request` has `shutdown`, `reboot`, `halt`, `reload` and `quiesce` but no restart (round 5, N-6).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/ospf/gr_restarter.go` `prepareRestart`, `grPrepare` - a planned graceful restart (Grace-LSAs, restart fact) runs only from `prepareRestart`, whose single caller `grPrepare` is the operator command `request ospf graceful-restart` (reason `grReasonReload`, 2); `config.go` `restarterEnabled()` is true only for planned restarter support, separate from the helper role (round 3, I-4). `prepareRestart` persists the restart fact (with `Expected`, the Full neighbours from `currentFullNeighbors()`), sets `gracefulStop`, then originates one Grace-LSA per interface; its comment: "The LSDB reliably retransmits the same instance; it is never re-originated" (round 4, I-7). `gr.go` declares `grReasonSoftwareRestart = 1` and `grReasonReload = 2` (round 4, I-8). Acknowledgement state (round 5, I-7): the retransmission lists are the unexported `LSDB.retransmit map[NeighborKey]map[types.LSAKey]*retransmitEntry` (`lsdb/lsdb.go`), written by the unexported `queueRetransmit` and `clearRetransmit` (`lsdb/flooding.go`, under `d.mu`); `NeighborKey` is `{Interface, RouterID}`; no exported accessor reads a list. A list also disappears without an acknowledgement: an interface teardown deletes every list whose `nbr.Interface` is that interface (`lsdb/link_scope.go`), and `removeFromAllRetransmit` drops a key from every list. `grOriginateGraceLSAs` returns interface names (`[]string`): on v3 only those whose `v6OriginateGraceLSA` returned true (a refused one is skipped with `continue`), on v2 every non-passive named interface it walks, because the v2 branch discards `OriginateOpaque`'s result; `prepareRestart` uses the slice only for the `graceLSAs` metric and returns only an error (round 7, I-2). `floodLink` (`lsdb/link_scope.go`) queues per neighbour and then sends the LSU in the same call, and the LSAck that clears the list is handled on the receive goroutine, so nothing records what was queued once it is acknowledged (round 7, I-3). The restart fact's `Expected` holds router IDs only (`currentFullNeighbors`, `gr_nvs.go` `Expected []string`); `hasFullNeighbor(iface, router)` (`gr.go`) answers per interface. OSPFv3 runs one engine, with its own `grManager`, per address family (`v6EngineSet`, `register_multiaf.go`; `show ospf ipv6 graceful-restart`), and `internal/plugins/ospf/v3` holds only packet, transport and types, so v2 and v3 share the `lsdb` package (round 5, N-8).
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/reactor/filter_delta_handlers.go` `attrModHandlersWithDefaults` - LOCAL_PREF uses the generic set handler; COMMUNITIES has no generic handler: `filter_community/register.go` registers it with `RegisterAttrModHandler(byte(attribute.AttrCommunity), ...)`, as LLGR's community add already relies on (round 2, N-5).
- [ ] `internal/component/bgp/wireu/wellknown.go` - GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN (0xFFFF0000) named, no sender anywhere.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/ospf/yang/ze-ospf-conf.yang` container `max-metric router-lsa` - `always|on-startup|on-shutdown`; `on-shutdown` range 0..86400, stored and reported. It originates no MaxLinkMetric Router-LSA, but it is not inert: `deriveRICapabilities` (`internal/plugins/ospf/ri.go`) already sets the RFC 7770 Router Information stub-router capability bit when `OnShutdownSec > 0` (N-5).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/ospf/instance.go` origination - the OSPFv3 path calls `v6OriginateSelf(cfg.RouterID, cfg.MaxMetric.RouterLSAAlways)` and the OSPFv2 path `lsdb.OriginateFromTopology(cfg.RouterID, cfg.MaxMetric.RouterLSAAlways)`: every re-origination (refresh, adjacency change, interface change) reads max-metric from config, so a drain set once would be overwritten by the next re-origination (I-9).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/isis/lsdb_wiring.go` - LSP generation takes `Overload: cfg.Overload` from config on every regeneration; same overwrite (I-9).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/ospf/ldp_sync.go` `ldpSyncManager` - RFC 5443 LDP-IGP sync, OSPF only; nothing under `internal/plugins/isis` implements it (A-7, I-8).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/vrrp/engine.go` `engine.teardown` - runs the worker's shutdown first "so a Master sends its Priority-0 advertisement (RFC 9568 Section 6.4.3) while its sockets are still open" (A-9, N-3).
- [ ] `pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_dispatch.go` `eventLoop`, `bridgeEventLoop` - one loop per plugin serves every callback serially, execute-command included (B-2).
- [ ] `internal/component/plugin/process/process.go` external spawn - `exec.CommandContext(p.ctx, plugin.Shell, "-c", p.config.Run)` with no `SysProcAttr`: no `Setpgid`, and context cancel sends SIGKILL to the shell; external plugins built on `sdk.SignalContext` (`pkg/plugin/sdk/signal.go`) end on SIGTERM (I-3).
- [ ] gokrazy supervisor, pinned module `github.com/gokrazy/gokrazy` `v0.0.0-20260703061218-a4a45a20149d` (`gokrazy/ze/builddir/github.com/gokrazy/gokrazy/go.mod`) - services start with `Setpgid: true`; `service.Signal` sends to `-pid` (the whole group); `killSupervisedServices` sends SIGTERM, waits `defaultSignalDelay` (100 s, "Allow for slow VM shutdown of 90 seconds, and then some", overridable per request by `wait_per_signal`), then SIGKILL. The reboot and update handlers in `update.go` run that sequence (A-5, I-2, I-3).
- [ ] `internal/appliance/cmd_push.go` - after writing the image, `target.Reboot(ctx)` through the gokrazy updater, unless `noReboot`: the upgrade reboot reaches ze as gokrazy's plain SIGTERM, not as `request reboot` (I-2, Q-9).
- [ ] `internal/plugins/isis/yang/ze-isis-conf.yang` leaf `overload` - boolean only, no timed form.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/fib/kernel/yang/ze-fib-conf.yang` leaf `flush-on-stop` - default false: Ze's kernel routes stay after stop. Unchanged by this spec.
- [ ] `internal/plugins/systemd/unit.go` `buildUnitFile` - `Type=simple`, `Restart=on-failure`, no `TimeoutStopSec`, no `KillMode`: systemd defaults 90 s and control-group kill.
- [ ] `pkg/plugin/sdk/sdk_callbacks.go` `OnBye`, `OnDoctorCheck` - callback pattern: one `callbacks[callbackX]` entry decoding an `rpc.XInput`.
- [ ] `internal/core/redistevents/registry.go` `RegisterProtocol`, `RegisterProducer`, `RegisterOSInstalled` - the route-source registry.
- [ ] `internal/core/ipc/yang/ze-system-cmd.yang` container `request` - `shutdown` ze:help says "drain connections" (it drains nothing today); `quiesce` is a test barrier.

**Route sources, from the registry** (`grep redistevents.RegisterProtocol`, test fake `fakeredist` excluded). This table is a reading of the registry on 2026-10-13, not the mechanism: it would go stale the day a new source registers, and that source would be silently undrained. The implementation therefore makes the drain choice part of the registration itself, and declares each fact once (round 2, I-7). A `redistevents` protocol registration declares exactly one of two values: `drained by <plugin name>`, a reference to the plugin whose own registration carries `OnDrain` and `DrainBudget` (the plugin registry holds the "drains" fact; the protocol registration only names where to look), or `no drain: <reason>`. `RegisterProtocol` is idempotent on name and "bgp" registers at two sites, so a repeated registration of a name MUST declare the same disposition, and `RegisterProtocol` panics at `init()` when it does not. The test lives where the real registrations run, in a package that links `internal/component/plugin/all`; it carries a control (the "ospf" and "bgp" protocols are present, so an empty registry fails) and resolves every `drained by` reference to a plugin name in the static `registry.Registration` set. It cannot check the handler and the budget: `OnDrain` and `DrainBudget` are Stage-1 runtime declarations, sent over the plugin connection and copied into `plugin.Registration` by `startup.go` the way `VerifyBudget` is, and the static `registry.Registration` (`registry/registry.go`) has no budget field (round 3, I-3). That half is checked where the declaration exists: after Stage 1, `startup.go` refuses a loaded plugin that a `drained by` disposition names and that declared no `OnDrain` or no `DrainBudget`, naming the protocol and the plugin. The refusal runs only for plugins that are loaded, so the functional tests prove it for every plugin some functional test loads, which covers the three `drained by` targets in this spec (bgp, ospf, isis); a `drained by` naming a plugin no functional test loads is checked by name only (round 4, N-9). Rejected: (a) a static drain fact on `registry.Registration`, because external plugins have no static registration and would still declare at Stage 1, so the fact would have two channels; (b) booting every named plugin to Stage 1 inside a unit test, which costs a plugin start per protocol to prove what every daemon start proves (AC-24). The column below is what each registration will declare:

| Protocol | Registered in | Drain in this spec |
|----------|---------------|--------------------|
| bgp | `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib.go` `bgpProtocolID`; `internal/component/bgp/redistribute/producer.go` `ProtocolID` (both MUST declare `drained by` the BGP plugin) | RFC 8326: re-advertise with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN the paths the drain verdict tags (Data Flow 5): every path to an eBGP peer, except a learned path to a route-server client; originated and eBGP-learned paths to iBGP peers, with LOCAL_PREF 0; reflected paths and learned paths to route-server clients stay untagged (RFC 8326 Section 4.2 scope, RFC 4456 Section 10 for LOCAL_PREF, RFC 7947 Section 2.2.4); wait, then Cease |
| bmp | `internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib.go` `bmpProtocolID` | none: monitored routes Ze receives, never advertised to a forwarding peer (A-8) |
| ospf | `internal/plugins/ospf/spf/install.go` `ospfProtocolID` | RFC 6987 MaxLinkMetric Router-LSA, plus raised metrics on its injected externals and stub networks (Q-8), for `drain-on-shutdown` seconds |
| isis | `internal/plugins/isis/spf/install.go` `isisProtocolID` | RFC 3787 overload bit in LSP 0, plus a raised metric on its advertised IP reachability that every receiver still routes on (Q-8, round 9 B-1), for `drain-on-shutdown` seconds |
| static | `internal/plugins/static/events/events.go` `ProtocolID` | none of its own; drained by BGP when redistributed into BGP (RFC 8326 Section 4.2 quote); when redistributed into OSPF or IS-IS, that IGP's drain raises its metric (Q-8) |
| connected | `internal/plugins/connected/events/events.go` `ProtocolID` (OS-installed) | none: kernel interface routes; carried by the BGP drain when redistributed |
| kernel | `internal/plugins/kernel/events/events.go` `ProtocolID` | none; carried by the BGP drain when redistributed |
| as112 | `internal/plugins/as112/events/events.go` `ProtocolID` | none of its own; its anycast prefixes are originated through BGP and tagged by the BGP drain |
| ipsec | `internal/component/ike/engine/redistribute.go` `ipsecProtocolID` (the tunnel routes `established.go` adds at SA establishment go out through `emitRouteAdd` under this protocol, round 2 N-3) | none: tunnel routes end with the SA; carried by the BGP drain when redistributed |
| l2tp | `internal/component/l2tp/events/events.go` `ProtocolID` | none: subscriber routes; carried by the BGP drain when redistributed |

Outside the registry but installing forwarding state (found by grepping netlink `RouteAdd`/`RouteReplace` and the MPLS installers; each is named in the drain doc page with its disposition, and none gets a drain of its own here). Each row's "What it installs" was read at its producer in design review round 2 (N-2, N-3, N-4):

| Installer | What it installs | Disposition |
|-----------|------------------|-------------|
| `internal/plugins/ldp/fib.go` | MPLS labels | follows the IGP path, so the IGP drain moves it (A-7, Q-7) |
| `internal/plugins/rsvpte` | MPLS TE labels | RFC 5817 gap (Q-7) |
| `internal/plugins/vrrp` | virtual addresses, not routes | Priority 0 at teardown already (A-9) |
| `internal/plugins/static/backend_linux.go` | static routes written to the kernel directly | none of its own; carried by the BGP drain when redistributed (static row above) |
| `internal/plugins/policyroute/rules_linux.go` | policy routing rules and tables | none: local policy, not advertised |
| `internal/plugins/iface/netlink/manage_linux.go` `AddRoute` | DHCPv4 default routes (`iface/dhcp/dhcp_v4_linux.go`, `0.0.0.0/0`), PPPoE client default routes (`component/iface/pppoe_client.go`), PPP IPv6 subscriber routes (`l2tp/ppp/ipv6_service.go`) | none: host and subscriber state, not advertised (advertised only if redistributed, then carried by that protocol's drain) |
| `internal/component/gtsm/route_linux.go` | GTSM hop-limit host routes | none: local session protection |

**Behavior to preserve:**
- Restart/reboot (`request reboot`, SSH `restart`/`reboot`): GR marker written, BGP sessions dropped in silence, no Cease, no GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN re-advertisement. The drain RPC carries kind `restart`; BGP answers it at once.
- After the drain, BGP still sends Cease / Administrative Shutdown within `shutdownNotifyBudget`.
- `flush-on-stop` false keeps kernel routes after stop.
- A commit during shutdown is refused with `errDaemonStopping`.
- `forceExitOnSignal` runs on its own channel that a `request shutdown`-injected SIGTERM or a SIGHUP does not trip.
- With no plugin configured to drain (all waits 0), stop time does not grow.

**Behavior to change:**
- New drain phase between `commitReloads.close()` and `gnmiSrv.Stop()`.
- `request shutdown`, `quit` (`handleDaemonQuit`), SSH `stop`, and the CLI shutdown function stop BGP through the hub (drain, then teardown), never by calling `r.Stop()` first.
- Signals after the stop begins follow the state table in Data Flow: a signal before the drain has finished skips or aborts the drain and the ordered teardown continues; a signal after that forces exit 1 as today. An operator facing a hung config fence therefore needs one signal more than today to force exit: the first skips the drain, the next forces (round 2, N-6).
- The single line `run` prints after `commitReloads.close()`, "Shutting down (Ctrl+C again to force)...", is false during the fence and the drain, where the next Ctrl+C skips or aborts the drain. It becomes one line per phase (signal state table, Data Flow) (round 2, N-6).
- The drain deadline is counted from the start of the stop, so a slow config fence spends from the ceiling.
- `eng.Stop` gets its own context, created after `apiServer.Stop()` returns.
- External plugin subprocesses start in their own process group; unit gains `TimeoutStopSec` and `KillMode=mixed`.
- OSPF `drain-on-shutdown`, new BGP and IS-IS waits originate their drain, and drain state is an input to every later origination until stop.

## Data Flow (MANDATORY - see `ai/rules/architecture.md`)

### Entry Point
| Entry | Today | Stop kind | Drain |
|-------|-------|-----------|-------|
| SIGTERM/SIGINT from systemd, gokrazy or a terminal | `stopCh` | `stop` | yes |
| `request shutdown` (`handleDaemonShutdown`) | `r.Stop()` first, then signal | `stop` | yes, routed through the hub |
| `quit` (`handleDaemonQuit`) | stack dump, `r.Stop()` first, then signal | `stop` | yes, routed through the hub after the stack dump (the dump is its only difference from shutdown) |
| plugin failure under the stop-daemon policy (`stopDaemonForPlugin`) | `shutdownFunc`, then signal | `stop` | yes for every drain-registered plugin with a live connection; the failed plugin has exited (`applyFailurePolicy` runs on exit), so the fan-out over live connections skips it with no exclusion parameter (round 2, N-11) |
| SSH exec `stop`, CLI shutdown | `r.Stop()` first, then signal | `stop` | yes, routed through the hub |
| `request reboot`, SSH `restart`/`reboot` | `stopForRestart()` (GR marker, `r.StopForRestart()`), then signal | `restart`, cause `in-place` (SSH `restart`) or `reboot` (`request reboot`, SSH `reboot`) (round 4, I-8) | RPC sent with kind `restart` and its cause; BGP answers at once |
| `ze push` appliance upgrade (`target.Reboot`, gokrazy SIGTERM) | plain SIGTERM | `stop` (Q-9 (u1), owner 2026-10-13): the upgrade reboot drains like any SIGTERM | drain, then Cease |

- Config: per-protocol wait leaves, verified at commit against the plugin's declared `DrainBudget`; Stage 1 `DrainBudget` verified against the daemon ceiling.

### Transformation Path
1. Stage 1: a plugin with an `OnDrain` handler declares `DrainBudget` (seconds) in its registration; `startup.go` refuses a budget above the ceiling or a budget without a handler.
2. Commit verify: each protocol's verify handler refuses its wait leaf above its declared budget.
3. Stop: `waitLoop` returns and the hub records the stop start time; it fences config (reload worker, confirm window, `commitReloads.close()`), reads the stop kind, and calls the plugin server's drain fan-out with a context whose deadline is the earlier of (stop start + ceiling) and (drain start + largest declared budget), cancelled by a signal per the state table below. A fence that has already spent the ceiling leaves the drain no time: the hub logs "drain skipped: stop deadline passed during config fence" and continues.
4. Fan-out: one `drain` RPC per drain-registered plugin, all at once, each carrying the stop kind, on kind `restart` its cause (`in-place` or `reboot`, round 4 I-8), and the deadline (no other field: round 9, I-5); the server collects replies and logs late or failed plugins by name. The calls overlap through long-lived server drain callers, never through a goroutine per stop (`CallRPC` blocks until the answer or its context ends, Required Reading):
   1. Caller lifecycle. `superviseProcess` (`server/failure_policy.go`), which runs one plugin generation's whole life once it reaches `StageRunning`, starts one drain caller on `s.wg` when that generation's Stage 1 registration declared a `DrainBudget`, and adds it to the server's caller set (keyed by process, under a server mutex) before it serves the generation's RPCs. When `handleSingleProcessCommandsRPC` returns, the generation has ended: `superviseProcess` removes the caller from the set, cancels the caller's context, waits for the caller to return, and only then runs `applyFailurePolicy`. A respawn is a new generation with a new caller, and the old one has already returned, so two callers never serve one plugin.
   2. Caller loop. The caller reads requests from its own channel, the stop slot (capacity 1), and returns when its context is cancelled. A stop request never waits behind a queued runtime request: `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` adds a runtime slot beside the stop slot and makes the caller take a waiting stop request before any runtime request, answering a runtime request still queued when it takes a stop request as refused without sending it. A call already in flight is not cancelled (a cancelled mux call drops its answer, below), so a stop that finds its caller mid-call is sent when that call returns, bounded by the drain deadline, past which the plugin is named late (runtime review r3, N-2). For each request it makes the blocking `drain` call under its own context, which lives as long as the generation, and never under the drain deadline: `MuxConn.CallRPC` deletes the pending entry when the call's context ends, so a call cancelled at the deadline would drop the answer. The call also returns when the connection closes (mux reader done, bridge callbacks stopped, connection read error). The caller posts the answer or the error to the reply channel the request carries, which has capacity 1, so the post never blocks when the fan-out has stopped reading.
   3. Queueing. The fan-out snapshots the caller set under the mutex (live generations only, so a plugin whose process has exited is not in it, AC-8), then sends one request per caller, each send selecting on the drain context and on the caller's done channel: a caller that returned before taking the request is logged as failed by name, and a send still blocked at the deadline counts as late.
   4. Collection. The fan-out reads the reply channels one after another under the one drain deadline, each read selecting on the reply channel, the deadline, the drain context and that caller's done channel. A send can succeed into the stop slot's buffer although the caller has already returned, because `select` picks at random among ready cases, and nothing then reads the request; the done channel is how collection learns it, so a caller that returned without posting a reply is logged as failed by name, never as late (runtime review r3, N-1). A reply by the deadline is logged with its elapsed time; a refusal or error is logged as failed by name; at the deadline every unread caller is named in the "late" warning; a cancelled drain context (signal, state table below) stops the reading at once ("drain aborted by signal").
   5. A late answer. The plugin's answer after the deadline still reaches its caller, which posts it to the abandoned reply channel and returns to its request channel; nothing reads it, and the stop has already moved on to teardown. Teardown closes every connection, so a call still waiting returns, `superviseProcess` sees the generation end, and the caller returns before `s.wg` is waited on: no caller outlives its generation.
5. Plugin: the SDK hands the drain to the connection's drain worker, so the plugin keeps answering commands and RPCs (B-2). On kind `restart` BGP replies at once. On kind `stop`:
   - BGP (redesigned in round 2, B-1, B-2, I-1, I-9). The handler runs these steps in order:
     1. No handler check. The drain rewrite is the session writer's own wire rewrite (the drain verdict below), modeled on the AIGP strip, so it needs no COMMUNITIES attribute-mod handler and has no refusal of that kind (redesign 2026-10-14: round 2's check, R-14 and AC-26 (b) are deleted with the per-rail drain step that needed `filter_community`'s handler).
     2. It sets the reactor's drain scope: one value owned by the reactor the BGP plugin runs, held behind one atomic pointer and replaced whole, never edited in place, so a reader sees `router` and `epoch` from the same change. The stop drain replaces it with `router` true and the next epoch, and the value also holds the scope it replaced (`replaced`, the walk's withhold below). From this point every UPDATE written passes the drain verdict below, because the verdict runs inside the writer, `Session.writeUpdateBody`, which loads the scope once per write inside `writeMu`. Wherever this spec says "the drain flag", it means the scope's `router` field, read with its `epoch` in one atomic load; "the flag is set" means `router` is true in the scope in force (cross-spec amendment, 2026-10-14: the scope with an epoch replaces a one-way flag so that `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` re-enters and leaves the drain on the same value).

        | Field | Type | Description |
        |-------|------|-------------|
        | `router` | boolean | whole-router drain; false until the stop drain sets it |
        | `epoch` | the pair (generation, counter), each an unsigned 64-bit integer, compared generation first | counter: 0 at reactor start; incremented by every scope change; never reset in the process. generation: set by the server, held in its drain record row and incremented when a respawned process takes the row (`spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain`, "Epoch lifetime"), passed to the BGP plugin in the `drain` RPC (`DrainInput`) and stored here by the change that RPC causes; 0 until a respawn, so under this spec alone the comparison is the counter's. The stop drain makes one change, so its epoch is (0, 1) for a process never respawned. Every refusal of an older walk in this spec compares the pair (runtime design 7, ISSUE-3) |
        | `replaced` | the scope's own fields (`router`), no `epoch` | the scope value this change replaced, read only by the withhold of a `drain`-kind replay and by its refresh demotion (below: a refresh only when the scope in force equals its `replaced`); the initial scope's is undrained, so only the initial scope equals its own `replaced` |
     3. It makes every write that preceded the flag visible to bgp-rib before the walk (round 3, B-2: sent events and the replay command reach bgp-rib on two different paths, so the order is made, not assumed). In order, under the drain context, holding no lock across a wait (`DrainEventsApplied`: "Callers MUST NOT hold locks needed by plugin handlers while waiting"):
        1. It resolves bgp-rib's process from the dispatcher registry by the drain-replay command's name, `request bgp rib drain-replay` (a hidden bgp-rib command, declared in `rib.go`'s `decls` beside `request bgp rib recovery` with `Hidden: true`, handled from a row of the `rib_commands.go` handler table, and kept out of `ze-rib-api.yang` as `request bgp rib recovery` is, because no operator calls it; round 4, I-9), the way `relay_recovery.go` resolves "request bgp rib recovery". A nil command or process is the "no Adj-RIB-Out replay" refusal below.
        2. For every Established peer it takes and releases `Session.writeMu` once: a per-peer `Session.writeMu` take-and-release, which the drain performs itself and which reads no send sequence (the rs-replacement spec deletes `recoverySnapshot` and `Peer.sentUpdateSequence` with its destination-wide fence): every write that completed before the flag, gated or forwarded, has then enqueued its sent event on bgp-rib's `eventChan`.
        3. It calls `DrainEventsApplied` on bgp-rib's process: a `Barrier` delivery through the same per-process FIFO as the sent events, which returns once bgp-rib has applied every event admitted before it, so `ribOut` holds every path written before the flag, each as its undrained egress body U (below). Before this call the handler reads the reactor's message-ID counter (`nextMsgID`'s source) once, after the last take-and-release: the cut M. A write whose sent receipt carries a message ID above M began its `writeMu` section after its destination's take-and-release, so it loaded the new scope and the peer already holds its drained form.
        4. It dispatches the drain replay: `request bgp rib drain-replay` runs the `outboundResend` walk over every up peer, and each replay command carries the existing `replay` meta key with the value `drain` instead of `true`, beside the sent-owner receipt (round 4, N-2: no new key and no new field). bgp-rib's `ribOut` is the replay source for both populations, forwarded and originated, because it is the only per-peer copy of what was sent that holds attributes (Current Behavior). The walk collects every peer's groups under `peerMu` held for writing, not the `RLock` `outboundResend` takes today, and sets bgp-rib's "snapshot taken for epoch E" state inside that same section, E being the scope epoch the handler passes as the command's first argument (round 4, N-7; cross-spec amendment, 2026-10-14: per epoch in place of the set-once "drain snapshot taken"). Its second argument is the cut M: the walk skips every `ribOut` entry whose `MsgID` is above M, because that write already went out under the new scope (step 3), so the peer is not sent a duplicate (RFC 4271 Section 9.2). The cut is the shape `relay_recovery.go`'s recovery request already uses (`request.Cut` against `SourceMessageID`, `rib_recovery.go`).
        No population is left in flight (redesign 2026-10-14). A body queued before the flag and written after it is judged at its write, under the scope it loads inside `writeMu`, so it goes out in its drained form whatever the scope was when it was queued; nothing is judged on a forward rail, so no item carries a judged epoch, no write is marked, and bgp-rib answers no event with a follow-up. `ribOut` stores U, the undrained egress body, for every write (sent event, below), so a write made under the old scope is in the snapshot in the same form as one made under the new scope, and the walk replays U for both. A replay older than a live write of the same key is refused by `allow`'s revision check for `adjOutExpected` (`session_ownership.go`), so a walk never overwrites a newer live write at the peer.
        When the drain-replay command has no owner (bgp-rib not loaded), the handler logs the refusal "bgp drain: no Adj-RIB-Out replay (bgp-rib is not loaded): routes already advertised keep their attributes; routes written during the drain carry GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN", and continues. When `DrainEventsApplied` returns an error, the handler logs "bgp drain: Adj-RIB-Out replay skipped:" followed by the returned error, does not dispatch the walk, and continues the stop as it does today: the drain wait, then the teardown (redesign round 1, I-3). A walk that bgp-rib refuses (an older epoch) or that returns an error is handled the same way: the handler logs "bgp drain: Adj-RIB-Out replay failed:" followed by the returned error, and continues to the wait and the teardown. In both cases the "drain finished" line (step 4 below) adds "Adj-RIB-Out replay not completed:" and the returned error, so it never reads as a completed re-advertisement, and the scope stays drained, so every live write until the Cease is still tagged (AC-36). The `DrainEventsApplied` error is a deadline, a retained earlier delivery failure ("The first delivery failure is retained for this Process lifetime ... Success never clears it"), or a stopped owner, and a fixed "deadline" text would be false for the last two (round 4, N-5; `ai/rules/user-facing-errors.md`).
     4. It waits its configured time or the deadline, then replies. The "drain finished" line reports, per peer, four counts, each in NLRI, the unit the writer counts (`updateSendCounts`), not in UPDATE bodies (round 5, N-9):
        1. Re-advertised: the announced NLRI the writer admitted for writes whose replay kind is `drain`. It is counted in `Session.writeUpdateBody` (`session_write.go`), under `writeMu`, beside `s.updateSendTotals.announcedRoutes += ownership.announcedRoutes`: when `s.sentMeta["replay"]` holds the kind `drain`, the same `ownership.announcedRoutes` is added to the peer's re-advertised drain counter. The count and the verdict read the same scope load (round 7, I-1, kept by the 2026-10-14 redesign): both run in `Session.writeUpdateBody`, which loads the scope once per write inside `writeMu`, so one load decides the verdict, the withheld count and the re-advertised count together, and a write cannot be judged under one scope and counted under another.
