The four checks that would overturn the write-time design were read at the producers: every UPDATE writer reaches `Session.writeUpdateBody` (`session_write.go`, `session_aigp.go`); every `ribOut` reader is fine with U except the Adj-RIB-Out show (runtime spec Q-8); the writer can carry U to the sent event (`notifyMessageReceiver` copies synchronously); the egress filters U passes are pure for the same input (`LLGREgressFilter`, `OTCEgressFilter`, `filter_community` `egressFilter` read no clock or counter) | `tmp/session/2026-10-12-0c3648c8-b9d7-45d8-b3f0-2473bceefb25/scratch/challenge-undrain-recompute.md`, re-read at the producers 2026-10-14 | - | `TestBGPDrainWriteTimeScope` | validated |
| A-19 | A drained write whose f(U) would exceed the destination's maximum, with f's growth g computed exactly for this write's cell and U, ends its ownership section with nothing written, copies U into its own pool handle, and splits that copy with the existing `message.Splitter` at the maximum minus g, each chunk written as its own write carrying its own U; on a split refusal the writer isolates only what f cannot fit: on `ErrNLRITooLarge` each NLRI longer than the space f leaves, that space taken from the refusal's typed `*NLRITooLargeError.MaxSize` (declared once in `message`) and never recomputed by the writer, is written alone (a pass that picks out none falls to the framing-refusal case), unchanged, warned and counted sent-untagged, and the rest of its section and every later section (the IPv4 NLRI after MP_REACH_NLRI included) are written as a new U, split again with f; on `ErrAttributesTooLarge` or `ErrMPOverheadTooLarge` that section alone is written unchanged, every NLRI of it untagged, and every other not-yet-emitted section is still tried with f; on a framing refusal (`ErrNLRIMalformed`, `nlrisplit.ErrUnsupported`, an MP parse error) the remainder is written with the split off and goes out untagged only when f(remainder) does not fit; so a route goes out untagged only when f leaves it no room in any message (redesign round 1, I-1; redesign round 2, I-1, I-2; redesign round 3, I-2) | read 2026-10-14: `ChunkMPNLRI` reports an oversized NLRI by its size (`w.tooLarge`), not its offset, and walks a section through `nlrisplit.GetWithdraw`'s per-NLRI `Splitter` (`chunk_mp_nlri.go`, `nlrisplit.go`), which the writer reuses to find the oversized NLRI; `chunkIPv4NLRI` is `ChunkMPNLRI` for IPv4 unicast (`update_split.go`); `writeOrdinaryUpdateBody` drives `message.GetSplitter` / `Splitter.Split` over `wire.ParseUpdateSections` and re-enters `writeRawUpdateBody` per chunk from outside any ownership section, and `writeUpdateWithoutAIGP` re-enters `writeUpdateBody` before `beginAdjOut` (`session_write.go`, `session_aigp.go`); `writeUpdateBody` copies the body into `writeBuf[HeaderLen:]` inside the section, and returns through `release` with nothing written when the filter admits nothing; `beginAdjOut` holds `s.adjOut.mu` until `release` (`session_ownership.go`); `splitUpdateIPv4` reuses `u.PathAttributes` and slices `u.NLRI` for every chunk, the MP path reads `mpReachInfo.value` from U; `splitUpdateWithMP` and `splitUpdateIPv4` emit the IPv4 withdrawn chunks before a later `ErrAttributesTooLarge` or MP error, each section's chunks are computed before the first is emitted, and the error set is `ErrAttributesTooLarge`, wrapped `ErrNLRITooLarge` / `ErrNLRIMalformed` / `nlrisplit.ErrUnsupported` / `ErrMPOverheadTooLarge`, and wrapped MP parse errors (`update_split.go`, `chunk_mp_nlri.go`); the raw forward path (`rawBodies`, `tryDirectWriteNoFlush`, recovery) reaches `writeRawUpdateBody` and never the ordinary split; `fwdBucketMerge` packs to `fwdBucketMaxBodySize`, the maximum minus the header (`forward_bucket.go`) | a split inside the ownership section deadlocks the destination on `s.adjOut.mu`; a split read from U in `writeBuf` corrupts chunks 2 to n; a refusal that writes the whole U again sends the emitted withdrawals twice; a refusal that writes the whole remainder unchanged sends untagged every route of the refused section and of every later section, though each would fit tagged in a split (redesign round 3, I-2); a writer that recomputes the splitter's space and leaves out the `highLen` double charge or the MP fixed fields gets a larger space, picks out no NLRI whose length lies between the two, falls to step 3 once (so it terminates), and writes the remainder unchanged, so the other NLRI go out without 65535:0 (redesign round 4, I-1; redesign round 5, I-2); a split of f(U) gives each chunk's sent event no U, so `ribOut` stores the drained form and a later re-advertisement under an undrained scope keeps 65535:0 for good | `TestBGPDrainGrowthNearMaxSplits` | validated at the producers (design); round 4 confirms the round-3 isolation, apart from the threshold, now taken from the typed refusal (`ChunkMPNLRI`'s `maxSize` is `maxMPAttrValue` less `mp.Len()-len(mp.NLRI)` for MP_REACH_NLRI and `maxSize - overhead - baseLen` for IPv4 NLRI, `update_split.go`; reported only as text today, `chunk_mp_nlri.go`) |
| A-17 | Every bgp-rib replay command's meta passes through the meta parse in `DispatchNLRIGroups` (`replayFromMeta`, `sentOwnerMessageFromMeta`, `initialReplayFromMeta` over `ctx.Meta`), the `update hex` commands included | `handleUpdateWire` (`update_wire.go`) returns `DispatchNLRIGroups(ctx, result.Groups)`; `cursor.go` ends there too; most `ribOut` replays are `update hex`, because `FormatAnnounceCommand` (`bgp/format.go`) writes `update hex attr set` whenever `RawAttrs` is set | a replay sent as `update hex` loses the drain marker | read in design review round 4 (N-1) | confirmed |
| A-18 | At the writer (redesign 2026-10-14; this row's basis was read for the former gate site and holds the same keying), a receipt replay's provenance is the destination's stored adj-out owner of the first key in the body that `allow` would admit (an entry whose revision equals the owner revision), keyed by the writer's own factored keying and section walk, and the writer can read it inside the ownership section `beginAdjOut` opened, before `ownership.filter` | `allow` refuses a key with no entry or a moved revision and only then sets `owner = entry.owner` for `adjOutExpected`; `adjOutWrite.key` strips the ADD-PATH path ID before `fwdPathKeyFor` (`session_ownership.go`; round 5, I-1, which found the round-4 text keyed with `fwdPathKeyFor` directly and took the first key with any entry); `adjOutPath.source` is a `*Peer`, nil only for a local advertisement (`adj_rib_out.go`); `beginAdjOut` takes `adjOut.mu` after `writeMu`, so the writer's read of `adjOut.mu`-guarded entries under `writeMu` keeps the lock order; `beginAdjOut` resets a table whose `session` is not the writing session, so the writer reads a table only when its `session` is this session and otherwise answers provenance none, uncounted (round 6, N-1); one body has one provenance (A-15) | the verdict would class a receipt replay from the wrong owner | `TestBGPDrainGateProvenanceFromAdjOut` (receipt of an originated path, of an eBGP-learned path, of an iBGP-learned path, of a path whose source session is down, a body whose first key has no entry, a body whose first key's entry a newer write replaced, an ADD-PATH destination, and a table still holding the previous session's entries, which answers provenance none and moves neither drain counter); round 5 confirmed the lock order and `sentForward` on every gate path (round 4, I-11; round 5, I-1) | unvalidated |

### Risks
| ID | Risk | Early signal | Mitigation / fallback |
|----|------|--------------|----------------------|
| R-1 | Lifecycle handlers changed to stop through the hub break an existing `.ci` that expects an immediate Cease on `request shutdown` | functional red in `test/plugin` or `test/reload` | the Cease still comes, after a 0-length drain by default (Q-3); update only assertions on timing, never weaken |
| R-2 | A rail sends a transit route untagged, or tags a reflected or route-server route | interop: FRR sees some prefixes without 65535:0; a reflected client sees LOCAL_PREF 0 | one verdict function run at the one writer every UPDATE reaches (`Session.writeUpdateBody`), never a per-rail edit; forward-rail tests run the real `forwardUpdateCore` and `reactorForwardRS` through to the writer and fail when a rail's item carries no source (redesign 2026-10-14); each writer reads an explicit provenance; one test per rail and per gated population (AC-9 list) asserts the verdict table; a destination whose `send-community` suppresses standard communities is answered by the verdict itself (Q-11), so no rail reports "tagged" for a path whose tag the mask removed (round 4, I-5) |
| R-3 | A signal during the fence or the drain is read by `forceExitOnSignal` and exits instead of skipping or aborting the drain | AC-6 test exit code 1 with no Cease | one goroutine owns `forceCh` from `waitLoop`'s return and follows the signal state table (Data Flow); tests cover a signal during the fence and `request shutdown` followed by one SIGTERM |
| R-4 | A plugin that never replies holds every stop to the ceiling | stop takes the full ceiling | deadline is min(ceiling, largest declared budget); late plugins named in a warning |
| R-5 | Fence + drain + teardown exceeds `TimeoutStopSec` (systemd) or 100 s (gokrazy) and the supervisor SIGKILLs | journal "State 'stop-sigterm' timed out" | the fence and the drain share one deadline, stop start + ceiling, so together they never exceed the ceiling unless the fence itself hangs (its reload `<-done` and `reloadGate.close()` waits are unbounded today and stay so: proceeding past an admitted reload would promote a candidate into a closed store, `run`'s comment at `commitReloads.close()`). An admitted commit reload can hold the fence up to its apply budget, which `capBudget` caps at `MaxBudgetSeconds` (600 s), above `TimeoutStopSec` 90 and gokrazy's 100 s: that stop is not bounded below the supervisor's kill, and the spec says so (Goal Validation, Known Limitations; round 2, I-8). Teardown after the drain: API shutdowns 3 s + `txShutdownGrace` 3 s + `pluginStopGrace` 3 s (BGP's 1 s `shutdownNotifyBudget` runs inside the plugin exit) + `eng.Stop` 3 s on its own context = 12 s, plus `gnmiSrv.Stop`. With ceiling 60 s and margin 30 s, 72 s fits 90 s; the unit test derives `TimeoutStopSec` from the constants |
| R-6 | Re-advertising a full table to every peer during the drain costs CPU and memory while budgets are short | perf of the drain on a large RIB | the replay is bgp-rib's existing cursor walk (`outboundResend` -> `resendRoutesWithCursor`), batched with delta encoding; the writer withholds every drain-replay path the verdict leaves untouched, so reflected and route-server paths cost no UPDATE. Withholding saves the UPDATE, not the cost of producing it: bgp-rib still formats, and the engine still parses and builds, one command per `ribOut` group per peer for every reflected or route-server path, to every client (`resendRoutesWithCursor` walks every group) (round 3, N-11). Measure on the Mac (perf runs there) before deciding whether bgp-rib should skip groups whose verdict is known to be untouched; that decision is not taken in this design |
| R-12 | On kind `restart`, BGP has already stopped in silence (`stopForRestart`) before the drain, so an OSPF (without GR) or IS-IS drain wait spends from the BGP peers' graceful-restart time | BGP peers flush the retained routes before ze is back | the operations page states that on a restart the IGP `drain-on-shutdown` waits run while BGP peers hold its routes as stale, and that the BGP restart-time must exceed those waits plus teardown and start; the waits stay bounded by the ceiling (round 2, N-8) |
| R-13 | bgp-rib is not loaded, so nothing replays what was already advertised | the "no Adj-RIB-Out replay" refusal in the log | logged refusal, never a silent untagged stop; paths written during the drain are still tagged; AC-26 |
| R-14 | Deleted 2026-10-14 (redesign): f is the writer's own wire rewrite and needs no COMMUNITIES attribute-mod handler | - | - |
| R-7 | A plugin crash and respawn during the drain re-advertises untagged routes | drain log shows a respawn | ProcessManager refuses respawn once draining begins (stop is terminal) |
| R-8 | `systemctl restart ze` now drains (it is stop + start) and lengthens restarts | operator report | documented; `restart`/`reboot` through Ze is the GR path without drain |
| R-9 | `KillMode=mixed` changes cleanup of external plugins | leftover processes after timeout | at timeout systemd SIGKILLs the whole cgroup in mixed mode too |
| R-10 | External plugins in their own process group escape a group-wide kill (gokrazy's SIGKILL to `-pid`, a terminal's Ctrl+C), and `exec.CommandContext`'s cancel SIGKILLs only the `sh -c` shell, not the plugin under it | `ps` shows plugin processes after ze exited | the plugin exits when its connection closes (A-12); ze's cancel signals the plugin's whole process group, not only the shell; AC-16 kills ze with SIGKILL and asserts the plugin is gone |
| R-11 | A re-origination during the drain (LSA refresh, adjacency change, a BGP session coming up) reads config and undoes the drain | FRR's LSDB loses metric 65535 mid-wait; a new BGP peer gets untagged routes | drain state is an input to every origination, not a one-time re-origination (AC-21, AC-22) |

## Blast Radius

| Question | Answer |
|----------|--------|
| What breaks if this is wrong? | stops hang to the ceiling, or peers see a Cease without drain (today's behaviour), or restart drains and the GR marker is contradicted by a Cease |
| How is it reverted? | single commit revert; installed units keep `TimeoutStopSec`/`KillMode`, which are harmless without the drain |
| Who else touches this path? | `plan/immediate/spec-ospf-max-metric-startup-shutdown-windows.md` (dependent); any spec editing the hub stop path or the `system.go` lifecycle handlers; `plan/immediate/spec-bgp-rs-replacement-on-withdrawal.md` (round 3, I-6), below |
| Shared with the rs-replacement spec | Files both specs edit: `internal/component/bgp/reactor/forward_rs.go`, `session_write.go`, `forward_pool.go`, `reactor_api_batch.go`, `internal/component/bgp/types/types.go`, `session_ownership.go` (this spec changes the two `replay` reads and factors the section walk and `adjOutWrite.key` for the writer's provenance lookup, round 4 N-2, round 5 I-1), and `docs/architecture/plugin/rib-storage-design.md`. This spec reads the rs spec's per-peer owed-refusal counter on `Peer` (`peer.go`, which both specs then edit). This spec also edits `peer_send.go`, `peer.go`, `peer_initial_sync.go` and `forward_bucket.go` (round 5, N-2, N-3). That spec also edits `relay_recovery.go` and `adj_rib_out.go`, which this one reads (the `DrainEventsApplied` shape, the `writeMu` take-and-release that `recoverySnapshot` performs and that the rs spec deletes with the function, and the `queueRecovery` -> `forwardUpdateCore` replacement write) and does not edit. Whichever lands second rebases its edits onto the other's in those files |
| Behaviour interaction with the rs-replacement spec | Under that spec an owner withdrawal on a non-ADD-PATH destination becomes an owed key that is not written: no sent event fires, so bgp-rib's `ribOut[destination]` still holds the dead path with its message ID and the adj-out entry keeps the old owner. A drain replay (receipt = that message) toward a destination whose verdict is tag would re-advertise the withdrawn path with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN during the owed window, a black hole (a refresh replay has the same hole). That spec's round 3 (B-1) rule closes it: an `adjOutExpected` receipt replay is refused while its key is owed. The drain replay is an `adjOutExpected` write, so the rule covers it and the drain replay checks nothing of its own. The replacement itself is written through `queueRecovery` -> `forwardUpdateCore` and is judged at the writer like every forward. Neither order of landing is required: if this spec lands first, no owed state exists yet; if the rs spec lands first, its refusal already covers the drain replay. The spec that lands second carries `TestDrainReplayRefusedAtOwedKey` (a drain-marked receipt replay during an owed window is refused, and the destination peer's owed-refusal counter, read as a before/after delta at the drain reply, counts it as a receipt replay refused at an owed key during the drain; the replacement written afterwards is tagged per the verdict). The counter is the one the rs spec defines (its round 6, B-1 option (i): a per-peer monotonic atomic on the destination's `Peer` in `peer.go`, staged by `adjOutWrite` during `filter`, one count per NLRI `allow` refuses with the owed-refused outcome, reset between the two filter passes, and added once per write by `writeUpdateBody` under `writeMu` with the table mutex at the three exits (the `length == 0` return, the PATHS-LIMIT `n == 0` return, the write path before `record`), on whichever goroutine writes, never reset, read only as a delta); this row names it and does not define it (round 5, I-6; checked against the rs spec's round-7 text in round 8, I-2). Reverse cross-reference (round 4, I-10): the main thread routed it to the rs spec's editor; the rs spec now names this spec in its Blast Radius ("Shared with the drain spec" row), and this spec does not edit that file |

## Wiring Test (MANDATORY -- NOT deferrable)

| Entry Point | → | Feature Code | Test |
|-------------|---|--------------|------|
| SIGTERM to daemon with a drain-registered test plugin | → | hub drain step -> server fan-out -> `OnDrain` | `test/plugin/drain-before-stop.ci` |
| `request shutdown` | → | `handleDaemonShutdown` -> hub drain | `test/plugin/drain-request-shutdown.ci` |
| `quit` | → | `handleDaemonQuit` -> stack dump -> hub drain | `test/plugin/drain-quit.ci` |
| plugin failure under the stop-daemon policy | → | `stopDaemonForPlugin` -> hub drain of the other plugins | `test/plugin/drain-plugin-failure-stop.ci` |
| SSH `restart` (an SSH exec client against a daemon whose config enables SSH, as the `cli-commit-*.ci` tests configure it; round 5, N-6) | → | `stopForRestart` sets stop kind restart -> drain RPC with kind `restart` -> BGP answers at once | `test/plugin/drain-restart-kind.ci` |
| second SIGTERM during drain | → | drain context cancelled, teardown continues | `test/plugin/drain-second-signal-aborts.ci` |
| SIGTERM during the config fence | → | drain marked skipped, fence continues | `test/plugin/drain-signal-during-fence.ci` |
| `request shutdown`, then one SIGTERM during the drain | → | drain aborted (first OS signal), Cease sent | `test/plugin/drain-request-shutdown-then-signal.ci` |
| SSH `show bgp` (the BGP summary) during a BGP drain | → | SDK drain worker; event loop still serving | `test/plugin/drain-show-answers.ci` |
| commit through the CLI during a held drain | → | `reloadGate` refusal after `commitReloads.close()` | `test/plugin/drain-commit-refused.ci` |
| `request shutdown` on a reactorless daemon (OSPF-only config) | → | `handleDaemonShutdown` `shutdownFunc` branch -> hub drain -> OSPF drain | `test/ospf/drain-request-shutdown-reactorless.ci` |
| drain plugins registered, every wait 0 | → | fan-out replies at once | `test/plugin/drain-zero-wait-fast.ci` |
| SSH `restart` with the OSPF restarter role enabled | → | OSPF drain handler, kind `restart` cause `in-place`, `restarterEnabled()` -> `prepareRestart` (reason 1) -> wait for Grace-LSA acknowledgement; no MaxLinkMetric (round 4, N-6) | `test/ospf/ospf-drain-restart-kind-gr.ci` (CLI and log); wire: interop `ospf-graceful-restart-on-restart-frr` |
| SIGTERM with OSPFv3 `drain-on-shutdown` set | → | OSPF drain handler -> `v6OriginateSelf` | `test/ospfv3/ospfv3-drain-on-shutdown.ci` (CLI and log); wire: interop `ospfv3-drain-on-shutdown-frr` |
| external plugin spawn | → | own process group; group SIGTERM reaches ze only | `TestExternalPluginOwnProcessGroup` |
| Stage 1 `DrainBudget` over ceiling | → | `startup.go` validation | `test/plugin/drain-budget-over-ceiling-refused.ci` |
| SIGTERM with BGP `drain-on-shutdown` set | → | BGP drain handler -> reactor egress rails | `test/plugin/bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain.ci` |
| SIGTERM with OSPF `drain-on-shutdown` set | → | OSPF drain handler -> Router-LSA origination | `test/ospf/ospf-drain-on-shutdown.ci` (CLI and log); wire: interop `ospf-drain-on-shutdown-frr` |
| SIGTERM with IS-IS `drain-on-shutdown` set | → | IS-IS drain handler -> LSP 0 origination | `test/isis/isis-drain-on-shutdown.ci` (CLI and log); wire: interop `isis-drain-on-shutdown-frr` |
| `ze systemd install` | → | `buildUnitFile` | `TestBuildUnitFileStopTimeoutDerivedFromCeiling` |

## Acceptance Criteria

| AC ID | Input / Condition | Expected Behavior |
|-------|-------------------|-------------------|
| AC-1 | SIGTERM; one plugin registered `DrainBudget` > 0 | the plugin receives `drain` while its connection is open, before any plugin connection closes; the daemon closes connections only after the plugin replies; the log carries "drain started" and "drain finished" with the plugin name and elapsed time |
| AC-2 | two drain plugins each waiting 2 s | the drain phase ends in under 3 s (parallel, through their two drain callers), not 4 s |
| AC-3 | a drain plugin that never replies, budget B; and one that replies after the deadline | teardown starts at min(B, ceiling) after drain start; a warning names the plugin; exit code 0; the late reply blocks no caller and is not logged as an answer within the deadline |
| AC-4 | Stage 1 registration with `DrainBudget` above the ceiling, or a budget with no `OnDrain` handler | plugin start refused; the error names the plugin, the budget and the ceiling |
| AC-5 | commit with OSPF `drain-on-shutdown`, BGP `drain-on-shutdown` or IS-IS `drain-on-shutdown` above that plugin's declared budget | commit refused at verify; the error names the leaf path, the value and the limit |
| AC-6 | each row of the signal state table (Data Flow): a SIGTERM or SIGINT during the drain after a SIGTERM stop; one SIGTERM during the drain after `request shutdown`; a SIGTERM during the config fence; a signal after the drain finished | during the drain: drain aborted at once, teardown continues, BGP Cease still sent, log "drain aborted by signal", exit 0; during the fence: no `drain` RPC, log "drain skipped by signal", Cease sent, exit 0; after the drain, or a second signal after a skip or abort: "forced exit", exit 1 as today |
| AC-7 | `request reboot`, SSH `restart`, SSH `reboot` | every drain-registered plugin receives `drain` with kind `restart`; BGP replies at once with no GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN re-advertisement and no Cease (unchanged GR path); a test plugin records the kind and the restart cause it received: `in-place` for SSH `restart`, `reboot` for `request reboot` and SSH `reboot` (round 4, I-8). SSH `restart` is driven in `.ci` by an SSH exec client against an SSH-configured daemon. The `reboot` half is proven by `TestHubStopKindRestartSentInDrain` only: `run` calls `reboot.Reboot()` after exit (`cmd/ze/hub/main.go`, `internal/core/reboot/reboot_linux.go`), which reboots the host when the run is root, as `./le test integration stress` runs (round 5, N-6) |
| AC-8 | `request shutdown` (BGP daemon and reactorless daemon), `quit`, SSH `stop`, and a plugin failure under the stop-daemon policy | each enters the hub with kind `stop`; the drain runs before any Cease; the peer receives NOTIFICATION 6/2 after the drain; the accepted answer is still written before teardown; `quit` still logs its goroutine dump first; on a plugin failure the failed plugin, whose process has exited, receives no `drain` and every other drain-registered plugin with a live connection does |
| AC-9 | BGP with `drain-on-shutdown N`, bgp-rib loaded; eBGP peers E1, E2, iBGP peers I1 (route-reflector client) and I2 (non-client); prefixes: one originated, one learned from E1, one learned from I2 and reflected to I1; a third config where Ze reflects to I1 with `next-hop self`, and E2's session carries `next-hop unchanged`; and a second config with Ze as route server for E1/E2 that also originates one prefix | on SIGTERM: E2 receives the originated, E1-learned and I2-learned prefixes again with 65535:0 and no LOCAL_PREF, including with `next-hop unchanged`; I1 receives the originated and E1-learned prefixes again with 65535:0 and LOCAL_PREF 0; I2 receives the originated and E1-learned prefixes again with 65535:0 and LOCAL_PREF 0 (round 2, N-7); the I2 prefix reflected to I1 is NOT re-sent and keeps its LOCAL_PREF, with `next-hop self` too; on the route-server config E2 receives no re-advertisement of E1's routes and receives Ze's originated prefix with 65535:0; after N seconds (or the deadline) every peer receives Cease 6/2. The whole matrix is proved from config through the real rails to the wire by multi-connection `.ci` tests with exact hex (`bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain-rr-matrix.ci`, `bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain-rr-next-hop.ci`, `bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain-rs-matrix.ci`), each asserting every tagged and untagged cell and the absence of an UPDATE for each withheld prefix (round 3, I-8). One unit test per call site and population asserts the verdict table there. The forward-rail tests run the real `forwardUpdateCore`, entered through `ForwardUpdatesDirect`, `RelayStoredRoute`, the ordinary forward and `queueRecovery`, and the real `reactorForwardRS`, each with a source whose provenance comes from `IsIBGP` (one source iBGP only through a migration AS, one unresolved), and each fails when the verdict is removed from `Session.writeUpdateBody` or the verdict stops reading the item's `writePath` owner (round 3, B-1, N-1; redesign 2026-10-14; redesign round 1, I-2). Through the gated writer: a local origination through `AnnounceNLRIBatch` (owner `originated`, authority unspecified), a config-static initial sync write (owner nil), a bgp-rib peer-up replay, a bgp-rib refresh replay (`sendRoutes`), an LLGR stale batch (verdict run once, not in `decideStaleReadvertise`; source peer down and still classed from its `*Peer`), a drain replay (paths whose input already carries what the verdict requires withheld and counted, round 5 I-4), a drain replay queued behind forwards through `queueBehindForwards` (its unchanged path still withheld, its provenance read from the adj-out owner, I-2, I-11), a write while `forwardFacts()` is nil (still judged, N-3), and a bgp-rs withdrawal (passed through untagged). `Session.SendAnnounce` has no reachable caller (`Peer.SendAnnounce` has no non-test caller, round 4 N-4), so it carries no AC cell; its write reaches the verdict by construction and asserted only in a unit subtest labelled test-only. Send-community (Q-11 (a), round 4 I-5): with E2's `send-community` excluding standard communities, E2 receives no drain re-advertisement (the drain replay is withheld and counted withheld, and the log names E2 and the setting); with I2's excluding them, I2 receives the originated and E1-learned prefixes again with LOCAL_PREF 0 and no 65535:0 |
| AC-10 | BGP with the wait at 0 or unset | no re-advertisement; Cease within today's budget |
| AC-11 | OSPF `drain-on-shutdown N`, OSPFv2 and OSPFv3 | on SIGTERM the Router-LSA is re-originated with every non-stub link cost 0xffff (v2: stub links, type 3, at 0xffff too, AC-32; v3: every interface metric in the Router-LSA at 0xffff, Router-referencing Intra-Area-Prefix-LSA prefixes at 0xffff, AC-32); injected externals at 0xfffffe (type 2) or 0x7fffff (type 1) (AC-31); adjacencies stay up N seconds; then teardown. Replaces AC-11 of the OSPF max-metric spec |
| AC-12 | IS-IS `drain-on-shutdown N` | on SIGTERM LSP 0 is re-originated with the overload bit set, and every LSP fragment's IP reachability at the drain metric (AC-33); the adjacency stays up N seconds |
| AC-13 | (a) no plugin registered a drain handler; (b) drain plugins registered, every configured wait 0 | (a) the fan-out sends no `drain` RPC and logs no "drain started"; (b) every plugin replies and the logged drain elapsed time is under 500 ms |
| AC-14 | commit attempted during the drain | refused with "daemon is shutting down; config reload refused" (existing fence) |
| AC-15 | generated systemd unit | carries `TimeoutStopSec` = ceiling + margin and `KillMode=mixed`; `ze doctor` reads the effective values with `systemctl show -p TimeoutStopUSec -p KillMode ze` (so a distro's `DefaultTimeoutStopSec` and drop-ins count) and warns, with a diagnostic code, when the stop timeout is below ceiling + margin or the kill mode is not mixed |
| AC-16 | an external (subprocess) drain plugin | it runs in a process group of its own (its pgid differs from ze's); a SIGTERM sent to ze's process group, as gokrazy and a terminal send it, reaches ze and not the plugin; the plugin receives `drain` and replies; after ze is SIGKILLed the plugin exits on its closed connection (A-12). The systemd half is AC-15 plus the A-4 run |
| AC-17 | a plugin respawn requested during the drain | refused; the plugin stays down until teardown |
| AC-18 | interop BGP against FRR | FRR shows the Ze path with community graceful-shutdown and moves best path to the alternate before the Cease arrives; red recorded with the drain reverted |
| AC-19 | interop OSPF against FRR ospfd | FRR's LSDB shows Ze's Router-LSA with metric 65535 on its non-stub links before the adjacency drops; red recorded with the drain reverted |
| AC-20 | interop IS-IS against FRR isisd | FRR shows Ze's LSP with the overload bit before the adjacency drops; red recorded with the drain reverted |
| AC-21 | a BGP session reaches Established during the drain | its initial table carries the drain tag per the AC-9 rule; at the drain's end it receives Cease 6/2 like every other peer |
| AC-22 | during an OSPF or IS-IS drain wait, an event that re-originates (LSA refresh forced by a test hook, an interface cost change, a new adjacency) | the re-originated Router-LSA keeps MaxLinkMetric and LSP 0 keeps the overload bit, and the injected externals, stub links, stub prefixes and IS-IS IP reachability keep their drain metrics (AC-31 to AC-33) |
| AC-23 | SSH `show bgp`, the BGP summary (and one plugin RPC) while the BGP drain waits N seconds | answered within 1 s; the drain still completes after N seconds |
| AC-24 | the `redistevents` registry as the full build links it (`internal/component/plugin/all`) | the registry holds "ospf" and "bgp" (control: an empty registry fails); every registered protocol declares `drained by <plugin>` or `no drain: <reason>`; every `drained by` names a plugin present in the static registry; at startup, a loaded plugin named by a `drained by` that declared no `OnDrain` or no `DrainBudget` at Stage 1 is refused, naming the protocol and the plugin; a test registration with no disposition, or a repeated name with a different disposition, fails naming it |
| AC-25 | SSH `restart` (kind `restart`) with (a) OSPF with the restarter role enabled (`restarterEnabled()`), (b) OSPF with the helper role only, or without graceful restart, (c) IS-IS | (a) a Grace-LSA per interface is originated with grace reason 1 (software restart) for SSH `restart` and 2 (software reload/upgrade) for `request reboot` and SSH `reboot`; the restart fact is recorded; on the v2 engine and on every running v3 address-family engine, OSPF replies only when every (interface, router ID) pair Full at `prepareRestart` has acknowledged that interface's Grace-LSA (off the neighbour's retransmission list while the neighbour is still Full) or has lost its adjacency, which is logged "adjacency lost before Grace-LSA acknowledgement", or at the drain deadline with a log line naming by interface and router ID each pair that had not (round 5, I-7, N-8); FRR's ospfd enters helper mode for Ze before the adjacency drops; no MaxLinkMetric Router-LSA is originated (round 4, I-7, I-8); (b) and (c) drain as on `stop`: MaxLinkMetric Router-LSA, overload bit in LSP 0, adjacency kept for the wait (round 2, I-5; round 3, I-4) |
| AC-26 | SIGTERM with BGP `drain-on-shutdown N`, bgp-rib not loaded | the log carries "bgp drain: no Adj-RIB-Out replay (bgp-rib is not loaded)", a path originated during the drain still reaches E2 with 65535:0, Cease after N s; not a silent untagged stop (round 2, B-1, N-5; the (b) cell for a missing COMMUNITIES handler is deleted with R-14, redesign 2026-10-14) |
| AC-27 | a write in flight at the moment the drain flag is set: (a) an originated path written just before the flag whose sent event is still queued for bgp-rib; (b) a forwarded path whose body was built on its rail before the flag and is written after the walk's snapshot, from the forward worker and from the route-server direct path `tryDirectWriteNoFlush`; (c) an originated item queued behind forwards and written after the flag; (d) a path written live after its destination's take-and-release and after the cut M is read; (e) a path received from an eBGP peer already carrying 65535:0 and forwarded to an eBGP peer before the flag | (a) the drain walk does not start until bgp-rib has applied that sent event, and the path is re-advertised tagged; (b) the writer tags it at write time, from the scope it loads, on both writers; its sent event carries U's attributes and `ribOut` holds U; no follow-up replay exists; (c) the writer tags it at write time and it is sent once; (d) the peer receives exactly one tagged UPDATE for it: the walk skips its `ribOut` entry, whose message ID is above M; (e) f changes nothing (U already carries 65535:0), so the drain replay of it is withheld, and no UPDATE carries 65535:0 twice; in every case no path written before the flag stays untagged until the Cease (round 3, B-2; redesign 2026-10-14) |
| AC-28 | a second stop-kind `drain` request (`stop` or `restart`) on a connection whose drain worker is in state `stopping`, the first drain still running or already answered | refused with an error naming the plugin, by the SDK event loop before the request is queued, so the refusal arrives while the first handler still waits (runtime review r2, I-3); one drain worker per connection, started once and returning when the connection closes; the first request moved the worker from `running` to `stopping` (round 3, N-9; cross-spec amendment, 2026-10-14) |
| AC-31 | OSPF `drain-on-shutdown N` with routes redistributed (static and connected) and `default-information originate`, OSPFv2 and OSPFv3, (a) with a normal-area attachment, (b) with an NSSA attachment, (c) as NSSA translator for another ASBR's Type-7, (d) with `drain-on-shutdown` 0, (e) with a redistributed route and `default-information originate` both at `metric-type 1` | on SIGTERM: (a) every type 2 Type-5 Ze injects, the default included, is re-originated with metric 0xfffffe, its E-bit, External Route Tag and forwarding address unchanged; (b) every type 2 Type-7 Ze injects likewise; the NSSA Type-7 default takes the drain value of its type (round 10, I-3): as NSSA ABR, which is this setup's case, `applyNSSADefaults` (`nssa.go`) originates it as type 1 at `a.DefaultCost` (`type2, metric, tag := false, a.DefaultCost, uint32(0)`), so it is re-originated at 0x7fffff; as a non-ABR with a redistributed default it takes its import's type through `externalParams`, 0xfffffe for type 2 and 0x7fffff for type 1; (c) the translated Type-5 keeps the Type-7's metric; (d) no external is re-originated; (e) every type 1 external Ze injects (Type-5 and Type-7, the default included) is re-originated with metric 0x7fffff, its E-bit (type 1), tag and forwarding address unchanged. A route redistributed during the wait is originated at the drain value of its type. Never 0xffffff (LSInfinity). Ze's own external SPF (`ComputeExternal`) at a receiver three links from Ze, each link at 0xffff, still installs the drained type 1 and type 2 routes through Ze when Ze is the only path, and prefers an alternate ASBR's route of the same type at its configured metric (Q-8; round 9 B-1, I-1) |
| AC-32 | OSPF `drain-on-shutdown N`, (a) OSPFv2 with a broadcast interface, a point-to-point interface and a point-to-multipoint interface, (b) OSPFv3, (c) `max-metric router-lsa always` with `drain-on-shutdown` 0 | (a) every type 3 link of the drained Router-LSA, the broadcast and point-to-point subnet stubs and the point-to-multipoint 255.255.255.255 host stub, carries 0xffff (round 9, I-3); (b) every prefix of Ze's Router-referencing Intra-Area-Prefix-LSA carries 0xffff, and a Network-referencing Intra-Area-Prefix-LSA Ze originates as DR keeps its metrics; (c) stub links and prefixes keep their cost (RFC 6987 Section 2) (Q-8) |
| AC-33 | IS-IS `drain-on-shutdown N` with connected, redistributed and leaked prefixes, IPv4 and IPv6, one leaked narrow entry | on SIGTERM every TLV 135 and TLV 236 entry in Ze's LSPs carries 0x7f000000 and the narrow TLV 128/130 entry carries 63; none exceeds those values; the stored prefix sets keep their configured metrics, and a regeneration during the wait keeps the raised ones. Ze's own SPF (`spf.BuildRoutes`, IPv4 and IPv6) at a receiver three links from Ze, each link at 0xfffffe, still installs every drained prefix through Ze, Ze's loopback included, when Ze is the only path, and prefers an alternate originator's copy at its configured metric (Q-8; round 9 B-1) |
| AC-34 | interop against FRR, ospfd, ospf6d and isisd | before the adjacency drops, FRR's database shows Ze's injected type 2 externals at 16777214 and type 1 externals at 8388607 (OSPFv2 and OSPFv3), Ze's OSPFv2 stub links and OSPFv3 Router-referencing prefixes at 65535, and Ze's IS-IS wide IP reachability at 2130706432 and narrow at 63; and, with Ze the only path to each, FRR's routing table (`show ip route`, `show ipv6 route`, `show isis route`) still holds every one of those prefixes with Ze as next hop, the type 2 and type 1 externals and the IS-IS wide and narrow prefixes each, which proves the drain steers rather than withdraws (round 9, I-2); red recorded for the database checks with the Q-8 raise reverted (the transit drain kept), and for the IS-IS routing-table checks with the wide drain value set to the withdrawing one (0xfe000000, which FRR's `process_N` drops at `dist > MAX_WIDE_PATH_METRIC` for any distance of 1 or more). The OSPF routing-table checks carry no FRR red (round 10, I-1): FRR does not cap a type 1 sum at LSInfinity (`ospf_ase.c` skips an LSA only when its own metric is `>= OSPF_LS_INFINITY`, then sets `new->cost = asbr_route->cost + metric`; `ospf6_asbr.c` likewise), so a type 1 external at 0xfffffe stays installed at FRR at any distance and that red would stay green. 0x7fffff is chosen for Ze's receiver and any receiver that caps the type 1 sum at LSInfinity, not for FRR; its discrimination is owned by the control of `TestOSPFDrainExternalStaysRoutable` at Ze's receiver |
| AC-35 | drain caller lifecycle: (a) a generation whose registration declared a `DrainBudget` reaches `StageRunning`; (b) a generation with no `DrainBudget`; (c) the generation ends while its caller is idle, and while its caller waits on a `drain` call; (d) a respawn after (c); (e) a full stop with two drain plugins | (a) exactly one caller is in the server's caller set for it; (b) none; (c) the caller is out of the set and has returned before `applyFailurePolicy` runs, in both cases; (d) the new generation has one new caller and the old one stays returned; (e) the drain starts no goroutine (the goroutine count during the drain equals the count before it, the callers already running), and after teardown no drain caller is running |
| AC-36 | SIGTERM with BGP `drain-on-shutdown N`, bgp-rib loaded, (a) bgp-rib's process holding a retained earlier delivery failure, so `DrainEventsApplied` returns it; (b) the drain-replay walk answering an error (redesign round 1, I-3) | (a) the log carries "bgp drain: Adj-RIB-Out replay skipped:" with the returned error, and no walk is dispatched; (b) the log carries "bgp drain: Adj-RIB-Out replay failed:" with the returned error; in both, the "drain finished" line carries "Adj-RIB-Out replay not completed:" with the error, a path written live during the wait still carries 65535:0, and every peer receives Cease 6/2 after N seconds, as without the failure |

## End-to-End User Stories

| # | User does | Path through system | Test proving it works |
|---|-----------|--------------------|-----------------------|
| 1 | `systemctl stop ze` on a router with BGP and OSPF drain configured | SIGTERM -> hub fence -> drain fan-out -> BGP/OSPF advertise -> wait -> teardown | `test/plugin/drain-before-stop.ci`, interop AC-18/AC-19 |
| 2 | `request shutdown` from the CLI | dispatcher -> shutdownFunc -> hub drain -> Cease | `test/plugin/drain-request-shutdown.ci` |
| 3 | operator presses Ctrl+C again during the drain | second signal -> drain abort -> teardown | `test/plugin/drain-second-signal-aborts.ci` |
| 4 | `restart` over SSH for a software upgrade | stopForRestart -> drain RPC kind `restart` -> BGP silent drop | `test/plugin/drain-restart-kind.ci` |
| 5 | operator sets a wait above the limit | commit verify refusal | `test/ospf/ospf-drain-on-shutdown-over-budget.ci` |

every other MP_REACH NLRI and every IPv4 NLRI goes out drained (65535:0 on each chunk, each chunk at most 4096 octets) and is counted re-advertised for a `drain`-kind write; the same with the oversized NLRI in the IPv4 NLRI section, and with two oversized NLRI in one section (each alone); an oversized MP_UNREACH NLRI goes out alone and counts nothing; an MP_REACH section with high-coded attributes (`highLen` above 0) holding one NLRI whose length lies in (the splitter's space, the space without the `highLen` double charge]: the writer reads the space from `*NLRITooLargeError.MaxSize` (asserted equal to the `maxSize` `ChunkMPNLRI` was called with), picks that NLRI out, sends it alone and the rest drained, and the write ends under the test deadline (redesign round 4, I-1); a refused section none of whose NLRI is longer than `MaxSize` falls to the framing-refusal path, written once, no loop; (ii) `ErrAttributesTooLarge` for the MP_REACH section (base attributes and MP overhead leave no MP value space with f) followed by IPv4 NLRI whose space with f is positive: the MP_REACH section goes out alone unchanged, warned, each of its NLRI counted sent-untagged, and the IPv4 NLRI go out drained; ### Boundary Tests (numeric inputs)
| Field | Range | Last Valid | Invalid Below | Invalid Above |
|-------|-------|------------|---------------|---------------|
| registration `DrainBudget` (s) | 0..ceiling | ceiling | negative, refused as malformed | ceiling + 1 |
| OSPF `drain-on-shutdown` (s) | 0..OSPF budget | budget | N/A (uint32) | budget + 1 |
| BGP `drain-on-shutdown` (s) | 0..BGP budget | budget | N/A (uint32) | budget + 1 |
| IS-IS `drain-on-shutdown` (s) | 0..IS-IS budget | budget | N/A (uint32) | budget + 1 |
| OSPF drain type 2 external metric (24-bit) | 0xfffffe only while draining | 0xfffffe (LSInfinity - 1; type 2 cost excludes the distance) | N/A | 0xffffff (LSInfinity) is never emitted |
| OSPF drain type 1 external metric (24-bit) | 0x7fffff only while draining | 0x7fffff (half of LSInfinity; distance up to 0x7fffff stays routable) | N/A | 0xfffffe is never emitted for type 1 (withdrawn at distance 1 or more) |
| IS-IS drain wide prefix metric (32-bit) | 0x7f000000 only while draining | 0x7f000000 (half of MAX_PATH_METRIC; distance below 0x7f000000 stays routable) | N/A | 0xfe000000 is never emitted (withdrawn at distance 1 or more) |
| IS-IS drain narrow prefix metric (6-bit) | 63 only while draining | 63 | N/A | 64 does not fit the field |

The OSPF and IS-IS rows above observe through the real entry points only: the daemon is started with its config on stdin (`ze -`), as `test/ospf/ospf-ldp-sync-show.ci` and `test/isis/isis-show.ci` run it, and the observation is made by an external drain fixture under `le test fixture`, itself drain-registered, which from its drain handler issues the show command over the plugin command path until the answer shows the drain state or its budget ends, and logs the answer for `expect=stderr`. No `.ci` asserts an OSPF or IS-IS packet: the `.ci` runner has no OSPF or IS-IS wire peer (its only wire expectation is `expect=bgp`), so every wire assertion and every assertion that needs a neighbour (AC-11's link metrics, AC-19, AC-20, AC-22's new adjacency, AC-25 (a)'s Grace-LSA and helper state) is made by the FRR interop scenarios below. The round-3 rows `ospf-drain-new-adjacency-keeps-max-metric` and `isis-drain-new-adjacency-keeps-overload` are removed for that reason: their AC-22 case moves into `ospf-drain-on-shutdown-frr` and `isis-drain-on-shutdown-frr` (round 4, I-6).

The reference shape ends with the observer's own `request shutdown`, so each row names its stop trigger (round 5, N-6, N-7). Every row runs the drain fixture's own entry beside `observeConfigured` (Files to Modify), whose drain handler calls `ReportFailure` with its verdict before it replies (round 6, N-4):

| Row | Stop trigger |
|-----|--------------|
| `drain-request-shutdown-reactorless` | the fixture's `request shutdown`, sent by the new drain fixture entry, not by `observeConfigured`: the observation happens in the drain handler after the ready-scenario verdict is already sent, so the handler reports through `ReportFailure` before its reply (round 6, N-4) |
| `ospf-drain-on-shutdown`, `ospfv3-drain-on-shutdown`, `isis-drain-on-shutdown` | SIGTERM: the daemon runs as `cmd=background` and the `.ci` sends `action=sigterm`, as `test/reload/signal-quit.ci` does; the fixture does not send `request shutdown` |
| `drain-restart-kind`, `ospf-drain-restart-kind-gr`, `ospf-drain-restart-kind-no-gr`, `isis-drain-restart-kind` | SSH exec `restart` from an SSH exec client, against a daemon whose config enables SSH as `test/plugin/cli-commit-*.ci` configure it; the dispatcher has no restart command, so the fixture cannot trigger it |

The OSPF rows inherit the constraint `ospf-ldp-sync-show.ci` records: the ospf suite does not provision the hub TLS acceptor natively, so they run only in the full CI / QEMU environment (and skip darwin). A native `./le test` run that does not run them is not evidence for AC-8, AC-11, AC-25 or AC-22, and the closing report names the environment each ran in (round 5, N-7).

Two ACs keep unit-only proof, each with its reason (round 3, N-10): AC-27, because the window it covers (a write in flight while the flag is set) cannot be held open from a `.ci` without a daemon test seam, so its tests hold bgp-rib's event application and the forward worker inside the real reactor and the real bgp-rib; AC-28, because the daemon sends one `drain` per connection by construction and only a hand-built client can send a second, which is what the SDK test is.

### Interop Tests (Scope: protocol)
| Scenario | Directory | Peer Daemon | What It Proves | Status |
|----------|-----------|-------------|----------------|--------|
| `bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/` | FRR bgpd, plus a second speaker giving FRR an alternate path | FRR receives 65535:0 (and LOCAL_PREF 0 on iBGP) and selects the alternate before the Cease; red recorded with the BGP drain reverted and the image rebuilt | |
| `ospf-drain-on-shutdown-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/` | FRR ospfd | FRR's LSDB shows Ze's Router-LSA metric 65535 on its non-stub links and on its stub links, and Ze's AS-External-LSAs for a redistributed static route at metric-type 2, metric 16777214, and for a second one at metric-type 1, metric 8388607 (`show ip ospf database external`), while the adjacency is still Full; FRR has no other path to either prefix, and its routing table (`show ip route`) still holds both through Ze as E2 and E1 during the wait (AC-11 v2, AC-19, AC-31, AC-32, AC-34; round 9 I-1, I-2); during the wait the check clears FRR's adjacency to Ze, and after it re-forms FRR still holds metric 65535 (AC-22, moved here from the removed `.ci` row, round 4 I-6); red recorded with the OSPF drain reverted, and a second red with only the Q-8 metric raise reverted (AC-34); the routing-table check has no withdrawing-value red, because FRR installs a type 1 external at 0xfffffe at any distance (AC-34, round 10 I-1) | |
| `ospfv3-drain-on-shutdown-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/` | FRR ospf6d | FRR's LSDB shows Ze's OSPFv3 Router-LSA interface metrics at 65535, the prefixes of Ze's Router-referencing Intra-Area-Prefix-LSA at 65535, and Ze's AS-External-LSAs for a redistributed static route at metric-type 2, metric 16777214, and for a second one at metric-type 1, metric 8388607, while the adjacency is Full; FRR has no other path to either prefix, and its routing table (`show ipv6 route`) still holds both through Ze during the wait (AC-11 v3, AC-32, AC-34; round 4 I-6, N-12; round 9 I-1, I-2); red recorded with the OSPF drain reverted, and a second red with only the Q-8 metric raise reverted (AC-34); the routing-table check has no withdrawing-value red, because ospf6d installs a type 1 external at 0xfffffe at any distance (AC-34, round 10 I-1) | |
| `ospf-graceful-restart-on-restart-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/` | FRR ospfd, helper enabled | SSH `restart` of Ze with the restarter role enabled: FRR receives Ze's Grace-LSA (reason 1), acknowledges it, and enters helper mode for Ze (`show ip ospf graceful-restart helper` JSON) before Ze's adjacency drops; Ze's log shows the drain reply after the acknowledgement; no 65535 Router-LSA (AC-25 (a); round 4 I-6, I-7). This is the first interop scenario with Ze as the OSPF restarter, so nothing existing serves as its template (round 5, I-10): `ospf-gr-frr` makes FRR the restarter and Ze the helper (its check runs `clear ip ospf process graceful-restart` on FRR, `check_extras.go`), `ospf-gr-fib-retention` only waits for Full, and no scenario drives Ze's `request ospf graceful-restart`. The scenario carries its own FRR helper configuration (`graceful-restart helper enable`, as the `ospf-gr-frr` frr.conf writes it) and a Ze config with the restarter role; SSH on 127.0.0.1:2222 and the user `interop` come from the lab, which injects them into every Ze config (`zeCLIConfig`, `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/prepare.go`), so the scenario's ze.conf carries no SSH block (round 6, N-3). Its check restarts Ze with an `opExec` in the Ze container of `ze signal --user interop restart`, the signal plugin's `restart`, which the SSH exec middleware routes to `stopForRestart`. The flag comes before the verb because `ze signal` parses with a `flag.FlagSet`, which stops at the first non-flag (`internal/plugins/signal/main.go`), so `ze signal restart --user interop` would leave `--user` unparsed. The lab holds no zefs super-admin, so the user comes from `--user` and the password from `ZE_SSH_PASSWORD`: `queryEnvironment` (`check_engine.go`), which `opExec` passes to `lab.Exec`, supplies it only when `command[1]` is `cli` today, and is widened to `signal` as well (round 6, I-3) | |
| `isis-drain-on-shutdown-frr` | `test/interop/scenarios/` | FRR isisd | FRR shows the overload bit on Ze's LSP, and Ze's extended IP reachability for its connected and redistributed prefixes, IPv4 and IPv6, at wide metric 2130706432 (`show isis database detail`), before the adjacency drops; and a narrow entry at 63: Ze originates narrow only for an entry leaked from a narrow source (`PrefixInfo.Narrow`, preserved by `spf/leak.go`), so the narrow case needs a second FRR container, `frr.conf` as a level-1 router with `metric-style narrow` advertising one prefix and `frr-sink.conf` as a level-2 router with `metric-style transition`, Ze level-1-2 between them; frr-sink sees Ze's leaked entry at 63 and routes the prefix through Ze. `prepare.go` starts an FRR container for `frr-sink.conf` (host `extendedSinkHost`) whenever the scenario directory carries one, with no BGP condition, and the shared daemons file enables isisd (round 10, item 3). frr-sink must be `metric-style transition` (round 10, I-2): FRR's `isis_spf_process_lsp` reads TLV 22 only when the area's `newmetric` is set and TLV 128/130 only when its `oldmetric` is set (`isis_nb_config.c`: narrow clears `new_metric`, wide clears `old_metric`), Ze originates no TLV 2 (`packet/tlv_neighbours.go`), so a narrow frr-sink cannot reach Ze as a node and a wide one ignores the TLV 128 entry. `frr.conf` stays level-1 `metric-style narrow`, which skips every TLV 135/236 prefix and Ze's TLV 22 (`if (!spftree->area->newmetric) goto end;`), so every routing-table assertion of this scenario, wide, IPv6, narrow and Ze's loopback, reads frr-sink (`check.Lab.Query(ctx, peerFRRSink, ...)`, as `check_extended_message.go` does); the database reads (`show isis database detail`) may read either. The overload bit does not hide Ze's IP reachability at frr-sink: FRR processes it outside the `no_overload` block, which gates IS neighbours only. Three routers share the lab's one scenario bridge (`prepare.go`), so every circuit is `broadcast`, on the `isis-lan-dis-frr` template, not the point-to-point `isis-max-metric-frr` or `isis-p2p-frr` (round 10, N-2). The narrow sums are pinned small, `frr.conf`'s prefix metric 10 and Ze's level-1 link metric 10, so the leaked narrow total, 20, stays below the 63 that `spf/leak.go` clamps a narrow entry to and the Q-8-reverted red goes red (round 10, N-3). frr-sink has no other path to any of them, and its routing table (`show isis route`, `show ip route`, `show ipv6 route`) still holds each through Ze during the wait, Ze's loopback included (AC-12, AC-20, AC-33, AC-34; round 9 B-1, I-2); during the wait the check clears FRR's adjacency to Ze, and after it re-forms Ze's LSP 0 still carries the overload bit (AC-22, round 4 I-6); red recorded with the IS-IS drain reverted, and a second red with only the Q-8 metric raise reverted (AC-34) | |

Each `stop` scenario stops Ze with the check engine's `opSignal` SIGTERM (`check_engine.go`, `lab.Signal`), which returns at once, not with `Lab.Stop` (`internal/le/interoplab/lab.go`): `Lab.Stop` is `docker.stopContainer` with a grace period and returns only when the container has stopped, and the engine runs its ops in sequence, so no op after it could poll FRR while the stop is in progress (round 5, I-9). After the signal, the check waits for Ze's "drain started" log line with kind `stop` (`opWaitLogContains`), polls FRR during the drain (`opWaitContains` or `opWaitJSONFields` over `vtysh` JSON), since the evidence is what FRR holds before the Cease, not after (N-4), and then waits for "drain finished" (`opWaitLogContains`). `ospf-graceful-restart-on-restart-frr` uses `opExec` of `ze signal --user interop restart` instead (its row). Docker's stop grace then applies only to the lab's final cleanup, whose timeout still exceeds N plus the teardown bound (R-5).

Interop check code: new `check_*` files under `internal/le/interoplab/bgp/` beside `check_ospf_interas.go` and `check_isis.go`; `check_isis.go` queries `peerFRR` only today, so the IS-IS drain check gains routing-table reads at `peerFRRSink` (round 10, I-2); and `check_engine.go`, whose `queryEnvironment` supplies `ZE_SSH_PASSWORD` for a `ze signal` command as it does for `ze cli` (round 6, I-3). Scenario directories are named, with no numeric prefix (`docs/architecture/testing/interop.md`); none of the five names exists under `test/interop/scenarios/` on 2026-10-13. Each red is recorded with `./le rfc discriminate-record` where the carrier holds an `RFC requirement:` tag (RFC 8326 after Q-4, RFC 6987, RFC 3787, RFC 3623 Section 2.1 for the acknowledgement wait).

### Goal Validation
| Goal (from Task) | Evidence |
|------------------|----------|
| Every route-adding protocol has a drain before stop | `TestRedistRegistryDeclaresDrain` iterating the live registry (AC-24), not the table above; BGP/OSPF/IS-IS handlers with interop AC-18..20; the installers outside the registry named with their disposition on the drain doc page |
| Traffic moves before the routes vanish | AC-18 interop: FRR best path moves to the alternate before the Cease, with timestamps |
| The drain and the teardown are bounded below the supervisor's kill; an admitted commit reload in the fence can still exceed it (Known Limitations, round 2 I-8) | AC-3 + AC-15 unit test deriving `TimeoutStopSec` from the constants; `TestDrainDeadlineCountedFromStopStart` |
| An impatient operator can still stop now | AC-6 functional test |
| GR restart is untouched | AC-7 functional test |

its HEAD descriptions and its Q-1 and Q-3 owner rows keep the old name); if that spec is implemented first it performs this rename, and this row then finds only the leaf move and its readers done (the next row owes the rest, round 9 I-4). ## Implementation Steps

1. **Phase: Wiring** - SDK `OnDrain` + `DrainBudget`, Stage 1 validation, server drain callers and the fan-out over them, hub drain step, test plugin
   - Tests: `test/plugin/drain-before-stop.ci`, `drain-budget-over-ceiling-refused.ci`, `TestDrainBudgetAboveCeilingRefused`
   - Verify: the test plugin receives `drain`; red before the hub step exists
2. **Phase: Stop kinds and signals** - route `request shutdown`/SSH `stop` through the hub, restart kind, second-signal abort, respawn refusal
   - Tests: the AC-6, AC-7, AC-8, AC-17 tests
3. **Phase: systemd** - unit constants and doctor check
   - Tests: `TestBuildUnitFileStopTimeoutDerivedFromCeiling`, `TestDoctorUnitStopTimeoutBelowCeiling`
4. **Phase: BGP drain** - YANG leaf, verify, drain scope and verdict, the `drainverdict` leaf package, f at `Session.writeUpdateBody` with provenance from the `sentForward` item's `writePath` owner and the adj-out owner (A-18), the near-maximum split by f's exact growth, from U's own pool copy after the ownership section ends, with the isolation after a split refusal (A-19: the oversized NLRI or the attributes-too-large section written alone, the rest split again with f; the remainder write only on a framing refusal), the send-community answer (Q-11), U on the sent event and in `ribOut`, the ordering sequence with the message-ID cut, the withhold by f's change, the `replay` kind (`replayFromMeta`, `NLRIBatch.Replay`, `session_ownership.go` readers), bgp-rib's hidden `request bgp rib drain-replay`, refusals and the walk-failure path (AC-36), wait, reply with the four counts
   - Tests: BGP unit tests, `bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain.ci`, interop `bgp-graceful-shutdown-drain-frr` with recorded red
   - AC-24 lands here, with BGP's `OnDrain` (main-thread decision under the owner's delegation, 2026-10-14): the `redistevents` disposition (`drained by <plugin>` / `no drain: <reason>`), `TestRedistRegistryDeclaresDrain`, `TestRegisterProtocolRepeatedNameSameDisposition` and the startup refusal of a `drained by` plugin with no `OnDrain` or no `DrainBudget`. OSPF and IS-IS add their `drained by` in phases 5 and 6, each with its own handler, so the refusal never fires on a protocol whose handler has not landed. `RegisterProtocol` in `internal/component/ike/engine/redistribute.go` belongs to the IKE-EAP work in flight: coordinate before editing it. Owner decision 2026-10-15: phase 4 does not start until that in-flight IKE work in `internal/component/ike/engine/redistribute.go` (`RegisterProtocol`) has landed.
5. **Phase: OSPF drain** - the Q-6 rename first (old leaf deleted, `ospf drain-on-shutdown` added, Files to Modify); `drain-on-shutdown` originates MaxLinkMetric (v2 and v3) and raises the injected externals, stub links and stub prefixes (Q-8); `show ospf` reports it; on kind `restart` with the restarter role, `prepareRestart` with the reason from the restart cause and the wait for Grace-LSA acknowledgements; if the dependent spec landed first, its interim refusal, its two refusal tests, its refused `.ci`, and the "refused" YANG and guide text are removed or rewritten per the Files to Modify row "OSPF interim refusal" (round 9, I-4)
   - Tests: OSPF unit tests, `.ci`, interop `ospf-drain-on-shutdown-frr`, `ospfv3-drain-on-shutdown-frr` and `ospf-graceful-restart-on-restart-frr` with recorded reds
6. **Phase: IS-IS drain** - `drain-on-shutdown`: overload bit in LSP 0 and the IP reachability drain metrics (Q-8)
   - Tests: IS-IS unit tests, `.ci`, interop `isis-drain-on-shutdown-frr` with recorded red
7. **Docs** - each phase edits its pages as it lands (Documentation checklist), not at the end

### Critical Review Checklist
| Check | What to verify for this spec |
|-------|------------------------------|
| Completeness | Every AC-N has an implementation at file:line |
| Correctness | on stop kind `restart` BGP emits neither tag nor Cease; the IGPs decide for themselves |
| Correctness | the verdict runs at `Session.writeUpdateBody` only, for every writer, with an explicit provenance, and no drain step remains in either egress chain or `exportFilterForBody`; `ribOut` holds U; no path written before the flag is missed by the walk (AC-27); the verdict runs once per write (never in `decideStaleReadvertise`); reflected paths and learned paths to route-server clients untagged; iBGP LOCAL_PREF 0, eBGP community only (LOCAL_PREF is not sent on eBGP); a drain-replay path whose input already carries what the verdict requires is withheld, and no tagged body carries 65535:0 twice (round 4, I-4; round 5, I-4); the provenance lookup at the writer uses `allow`'s own keying and revision test (round 5, I-1); the drain counters are in NLRI and count only what the writer admitted, nothing refused by the next-hop withhold, `allow` or an owed key (round 5, I-5); a destination with standard communities suppressed is answered per Q-11; the scope is loaded once per write inside `writeMu` and the shared `fwdItem.meta` is never written |
| Correctness | the idle drain step costs one atomic load per destination per forward and allocates nothing |
| Correctness | the signal state table holds cell by cell; the drain deadline is counted from stop start |
| Naming | `drain-budget` JSON kebab-case; one leaf name, `drain-on-shutdown`, under bgp, ospf and isis (Q-6) |
| Data flow | hub and server never name a protocol; the ceiling is declared once |
| Rule: goroutine-lifecycle | the fan-out starts no goroutine: it queues to one long-lived server drain caller per drain-registered generation, which reads its request channel, is started by `superviseProcess` with the generation, returns when the generation ends and is waited for before `applyFailurePolicy`, and posts each answer, a late one included, to a capacity-1 reply channel so it never blocks (AC-35); the SDK drain handler runs on one long-lived drain worker per connection reading a channel, never on a goroutine started per request |
| Rule: rfc-compliance | RFC 8326 Section 4.2, RFC 6987 Section 2, RFC 3787 quotes above the implementing functions |
