### Failure Routing

| Failure | Route To |
|---------|----------|
| Compilation error | Fix in the phase that introduced it |
| Test fails for the wrong reason | Fix the test assertion or setup |
| Test fails on behavior mismatch | Re-read the source in Current Behavior. If misunderstood → RESEARCH |
| Lint failure | Fix inline. If architectural → DESIGN |
| Functional test fails | Check the AC: wrong AC → DESIGN, correct AC → IMPLEMENT |
| Audit finds a missing AC | Back to the relevant phase and implement |
| 3 fix attempts failed | STOP. Report all 3 approaches. Ask the user |

## Design Insights
- No open-source routing daemon checked drains on SIGTERM (FRR, BIRD, GoBGP, holo, OpenBGPD); commercial NOSes drain through an operator maintenance command. FRR had stop-time OSPF drain in 2005 and removed it for kill in 2023 (research §1.2). The ceiling and the second-signal abort answer why FRR backed away: a supervisor that stops waiting.
- The stop path has more than one Cease producer today: the lifecycle handlers' direct `r.Stop()` (`handleDaemonShutdown`, `handleDaemonQuit`, SSH `stop`) and the plugin exit's `bgpReactor.Stop()`. The drain needs one, the last.
- A per-rail edit is the shape that failed before: `Reactor.Stop`'s comment records that "the rails are found by inspection and the list is never finished". Rounds 2 and 3 answered it with a drain step in each egress chain plus a call in the originated-route gate, three call sites that each had to be found (B-3; round 3, B-1). The redesign of 2026-10-14 removes the list altogether: every rail ends at one writer, `Session.writeUpdateBody`, so the verdict and f run there once, and a new rail reaches the drain by reaching the writer. The writer is also where the scope is read at the moment of sending, so nothing is judged early and no in-flight window exists. Provenance is the one input the writer does not compute itself, and it already holds it: a forward item's `writePath` owner (`receivedPeer` or the span's source), and for the gated writer the write's owner and the adj-out owner (round 2, B-2; redesign round 1, I-2).
- Round 1 reasoned from the reactor's Adj-RIB-Out, and round 2 found it holds no attributes. The replay source was then chosen by asking which component holds what was sent, with attributes, per peer: only bgp-rib's `ribOut` does (round 2, B-1).

## Key Design Decisions
| Decision | Alternatives Considered | Rationale |
|----------|------------------------|-----------|
| Daemon-wide drain RPC, plugins opt in | (b) each plugin drains on its own `bye`/connection close with a longer `pluginStopGrace` | (b) runs after connections close, so nothing is delivered; it also makes every plugin's grace the daemon's stop time, with no ceiling and no abort |
| Static `DrainBudget` declared at Stage 1, plus per-protocol wait leaves verified against it | (b) the plugin reports its configured wait to the daemon at each apply; (c) the daemon reads every protocol's config; (d) drop the field, every leaf bounded by the ceiling | (b) adds a second runtime channel for one number; (c) makes the core name protocol config. A static budget gives the daemon its deadline without config knowledge. Values (N-2): BGP, OSPF and IS-IS each declare `DrainBudget` equal to the ceiling, so their wait leaves are bounded by the ceiling; the drain test plugin declares 2 s, which is what lets AC-2 and AC-3 run in seconds rather than at the ceiling, and is why (d) is not taken |
| Drain RPC carries the stop kind; each plugin decides | the hub skips the drain on restart for every plugin | only BGP has a protocol reason to stay silent on restart (RFC 4724 Section 5); an IGP without graceful restart loses its adjacencies on a restart just as on a stop (I-2) |
| One drain verdict function of (provenance, destination class, mask, scope), applied as f(U, scope) at the one writer every UPDATE reaches, `Session.writeUpdateBody`, before `ownership.filter`; `ribOut` stores U (redesign 2026-10-14, `tmp/session/2026-10-12-0c3648c8-b9d7-45d8-b3f0-2473bceefb25/scratch/challenge-undrain-recompute.md`) | (b) a filter registered through `filterapi.Register` with `Readvertise` (round 1's design); (c) tag on each rail; (d) round 3's design: one reactor-owned drain step in both forward chains (`r.orderedEgressSteps` at `FilterStageDrain`, after the route-server rail's `r.egressFilters` loop) and in `exportFilterForBody`; (e) round 2's design, one step appended to `r.egressFilters` only; (f) a `filterapi.EgressFilterFunc` entry in both chains | (b) is init-only and process-wide while the flag is per reactor, it runs twice on an LLGR stale batch, and the stale rail gives it a zero source (round 2, B-2, I-9); (c) is the list that is never finished; (d) judges a forwarded body on its rail, before the write, so a body judged under one scope and written under another needed a judged epoch, a per-write mark and a bgp-rib follow-up, and it recorded f's output in `ribOut`, which f cannot be inverted from (it loses "65535:0 already present" and the LOCAL_PREF it replaced): the runtime undrain then needed per-key records, an epoch table and fallbacks, and five review rounds kept finding holes in them. Round 3 rejected the writer because "the final writers are several and some write pre-encoded raw bodies"; read again on 2026-10-14, every writer ends in `writeUpdateBody`, which already rewrites the body in the session buffer (AIGP strip, `clearTransmittedLabelIndex`), so f is the same kind of rewrite, not a re-encode (B-3 answered); (e) reaches only the route-server rail (round 3, B-1); (f) sees only `PeerFilterInfo`, so it would derive iBGP-ness from AS numbers (round 3, N-1) |
| Ordering of the drain walk against writes in flight: per-peer `writeMu` take-and-release, the message-ID cut, `DrainEventsApplied` on bgp-rib's process, dispatch | (b) assume one ordered path (round 2's A-14); (c) a drain-barrier event emitted by the reactor, which bgp-rib acknowledges before walking; (d) `fwdPool.Barrier` after the flag; (e) round 4's `drain-epoch` follow-up for bodies judged on a rail in an older epoch | (b) is false: sent events go fire-and-forget through `deliveryLoop`, the command through dispatch (round 3, B-2); (c) is what `DrainEventsApplied` already is, and `relay_recovery.go` already relies on it before a RIB command; (d) is not needed for the form, since every write computes it under the scope in force; (e) answered a population the write-time verdict no longer has (no body is judged before its write), so its meta key, the judged epoch on `fwdItem`, both writers' marks and the bgp-rib branch are deleted. The cut keeps a live drained write from being re-sent by the walk (RFC 4271 Section 9.2) for the cost of one integer on the walk command |
| Provenance at the gate from `Session.sentForward` and the destination's adj-out owner (A-18) | round 3's design: bgp-rib adds its group's provenance (`SourceLocalMeta` or `source-peer`) to every `replay` command, `update_text.go` parses it into `NLRIBatch`, `sendBatchUpdate` and `queueBehindForwards` copy it into `fwdItem`, and the `egressRouteFilter` closure type gains a provenance argument | round 3's channel is a second copy of a fact the reactor holds: for a receipt `allow` already sets `owner = entry.owner`, and `adjOutPath.source` is the source `*Peer` (nil for a local advertisement), so the reactor's record answers and bgp-rib's would be a second declaration that could disagree (`ai/rules/principles.md`, declared once). Taking it from the reactor removes the meta keys, their parse, the `NLRIBatch` and `fwdItem` fields, the `queueBehindForwards` copy, the closure signature change and `TestRIBReplayCommandsCarryProvenance`, and classes a down source from its `*Peer` with no lookup (round 3 N-2). Its cost is one adj-out lookup per receipt write while draining (round 4, I-11) |
| The drain marker is the value `drain` of the existing `replay` meta key | a new meta key and an `NLRIBatch` field beside `Replay` | `replayFromMeta`, `NLRIBatch.Replay`, `queueBehindForwards(..., replay)` and `item.meta{"replay": ...}` already carry the key end to end, and bgp-rib tests it by presence, so a second key would be a parallel channel for "this is a replay, of this kind"; the cost is that the reactor's bool readers (two in `session_ownership.go`, `replayFromMeta`) become kind readers (round 4, N-2) |
| The grace reason comes from a protocol-neutral restart cause in the drain RPC (`in-place`, `reboot`) | reason 1 for every restart (round 3); reason 2 for every restart, as `request ospf graceful-restart` | reason 1 for a reboot is refused by a helper under RFC 3623 Section 3.1 policy (c); reason 2 for SSH `restart` misreports an in-place restart; the hub already knows which entry point it serves (`rebootRequested`) and passes the cause, not an OSPF code, so it names no protocol (round 4, I-8) |
| Send-community honoured by the verdict (Q-11 (a)) | override the mask during the drain (Q-11 (b)) | see Q-11; under (a) the counters and the log state what was sent (round 4, I-5) |
| The drain marker with withholding is kept; the `drain-epoch` follow-up is deleted (redesign 2026-10-14) | drop the marker | without the marker and withholding the writer re-sends reflected and route-server paths unchanged (RFC 4271 Section 9.2, AC-9's "NOT re-sent" cells). The follow-up's AC-27 (b) is met by the write-time verdict itself, so deleting it cuts no scope (round 4, N-11 kept for the marker) |
| `drained by` resolved by name in a static test and by declaration at Stage 1 | (a) a static drain fact on `registry.Registration`; (b) boot each named plugin to Stage 1 in a unit test | see the route-source paragraph (round 3, I-3) |
| Verdict keyed on provenance and destination, per RFC 8326 Section 4.2's scope | (b) key on whether the copy's effective next hop is Ze (round 2, I-1); (c) tag every advertised route | (b) leaves an eBGP-learned path to iBGP with its next hop unchanged untagged, which Section 4.2's inbound bullet tags, and leaves a `next-hop unchanged` path to an eBGP peer untagged, which the outbound bullet tags ("the paths propagated over the session"); (c) tags reflected paths, whose LOCAL_PREF RFC 4456 Section 10 says a reflector SHOULD NOT modify, and route-server paths, whose communities RFC 7947 Section 2.2.4 says SHOULD NOT be modified |
| Drain re-advertisement replays bgp-rib's `ribOut` through its existing `outboundResend` walk, with a drain marker, after the reactor flag is set | (b) replay the reactor's `adjRIBOut` (round 1); (c) per population: forwarded routes from bgp-adj-rib-in through `RelayStoredRoute`, originated ones from their owners | (b) holds no attributes ("adjRIBOutRoute retains no forwarded attributes") and has no reader outside the final writer (round 2, B-1); (c) is two replays with two owners, and bgp-adj-rib-in holds every received path, not what each peer was sent; `ribOut` alone holds the sent attributes and provenance of both populations. The round-1 fear that suppression would swallow the replay was wrong: suppression only matches a byte-equal local body and `replay` already bypasses it |
| The gate withholds a drain-replay path the verdict leaves untouched | re-send every `ribOut` path unchanged | RFC 4271 Section 9.2: a speaker "SHOULD NOT advertise a given feasible BGP route from its Adj-RIB-Out if it would produce an UPDATE message containing the same BGP route as was previously advertised"; withholding also spares a full table to every reflector client |
| A missing replay owner, a failed `DrainEventsApplied` or a failed walk is a logged refusal naming the error, and the stop proceeds (AC-26, AC-36); the tag needs no COMMUNITIES handler, because f is the writer's own rewrite | stop untagged in silence | `ai/rules/principles.md`: code that cannot answer says so (round 2, B-1, N-5) |
| Q-9 (u1), owner 2026-10-13: the upgrade reboot drains and sends Cease | (u2) arm the restart kind for the next SIGTERM; ze exits 125 after a restart-kind stop | (u2) needs a daemon verb, an SSH client in `ze push`, an arm expiry and GR marker removal, because `ze push` reaches only the gokrazy updater (round 3, I-5); exit 125 leaves ze down after a failed reboot (round 2, I-4). The owner chose (u1) |
| The fan-out queues to one long-lived server drain caller per drain-registered generation, started and ended by `superviseProcess`, each call made under the caller's own context; the deadline bounds only the collection | (b) one goroutine per draining plugin per stop (this spec before 2026-10-14); (c) sequential calls on the hub's goroutine under the shared deadline; (d) each call made under the drain deadline | `CallRPC` blocks until the answer or its context ends (mux, `Conn`, bridge, `PluginConn`, read 2026-10-14). (c) lets a slow plugin spend the deadline before later plugins are even sent the request, breaking AC-2; (b) works for a stop alone but is a goroutine started per request, which `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` cannot reuse for a command that repeats, and building (b) now would be replaced there (`ai/rules/no-layering.md`), so the reusable shape is built once (main-thread decision, same reasoning as runtime review r1 I-1); (d) drops a late answer, because a cancelled mux call deletes its pending entry |
| Drain deadline counted from stop start | from drain start | the fence before the drain is unbounded today, so a deadline from drain start does not bound the stop (I-5) |
| External plugins in their own process group | ignore SIGTERM in the SDK while connected | a plugin not built on the SDK, or one that installs its own handler, would still die; a process group is set by the spawner once for all of them (I-3) |
| `quit` drains like `shutdown` | `quit` skips the drain | the stack dump is `quit`'s only difference; an operator who wants no wait has the signal table (I-6) |
| Ceiling as one constant in `pkg/plugin/rpc`, unit `TimeoutStopSec` derived from it | config leaf | the unit is generated at install time; a configurable ceiling drifts (Q-1) |
| Lifecycle handlers stop BGP through the hub | keep `r.Stop()` and drain inside the handler | two drain entry points; SIGTERM would still need the hub one |
| Drain between `commitReloads.close()` and `gnmiSrv.Stop()` | before the config fence | a commit landing during the drain could change the config being drained |

## Known Limitations
- Drain on protocol deletion by commit: Q-5 (a) keeps it out.
- RSVP-TE RFC 5817 graceful shutdown: a conformance gap (Q-7). Its implementation is its own spec, `plan/spec-rsvp-te-graceful-shutdown-rfc5817.md` (owner, 2026-10-13: "make a note to eventually implement RFC 5817 - not urgent").
- The NSSA translator's Type-5 keeps the metric of the Type-7 it translates (Q-8 boundary, confirmed by design review round 9, N-2).
- A drained type 1 external cannot steer onto a type 2 alternate. RFC 2328 Section 16.4 step (6) (b): "Type 1 external paths are always preferred over type 2 external paths." (The review cited this as Section 16.4.1 (b); the sentence is in Section 16.4 step (6), and Section 16.4.1 holds the intra-AS path preferences.) Ze's receiver applies it by rank before metric (`betterExternal`, `routeTypeRank`, `spf/external.go`). Raising a type 1 metric to 0x7fffff moves traffic onto another type 1 path only; a receiver whose alternate is a type 2 external keeps Ze's drained type 1 route until the adjacency drops. Changing the E-bit during the drain would steer onto such an alternate, but it rewrites an attribute the drain otherwise keeps and is not in this design (round 9, I-1).
- The drain values leave room for any realistic distance, not any distance: a receiver more than 0x7fffff from Ze (OSPF type 1) or 0x7f000000 (IS-IS wide) withdraws the drained route, and an alternate costlier than the drain value plus the distance to Ze still loses to it. Both bounds need well over a hundred links at the maximum metric (Data Flow, "Injected prefixes").
- FRR (ospfd and ospf6d, `frr-10.3.1`) keeps a type 1 external at any sum below 2^32: it skips an LSA only when the LSA's own metric is LSInfinity and adds the distance with no cap (`ospf_ase.c` `new->cost = asbr_route->cost + metric`, `ospf6_asbr.c` likewise). The OSPF type 1 bound above, and the choice of 0x7fffff over 0xfffffe, are for Ze's receiver and any receiver that caps the type 1 sum at LSInfinity, not for FRR, and the interop OSPF routing-table checks therefore carry no withdrawing-value red (AC-34, round 10 I-1).
- RFC 8326 receiver side (inbound LOCAL_PREF on received GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN): not in this spec (Q-4).
- RFC 8326 Section 4 SHOULD ("The LOCAL_PREF value SHOULD be lower than any of the alternative paths"): met at every iBGP receiver by LOCAL_PREF 0 on the tagged copies; Ze's own Loc-RIB keeps the path's LOCAL_PREF, so Ze's own transit traffic keeps the exit until the Cease. Recorded against Section 4, with Section 4.2's inbound bullet as the substituted procedure (Q-10).
- Reflected and route-server routes are not tagged (B-1); their clients lose them at the Cease unless a redundant reflector or route server holds them. This holds for a reflected path whose next hop Ze set to itself (`next-hop self`), whose traffic does cross Ze: tagging it needs LOCAL_PREF 0, which RFC 4456 Section 10 says a reflector SHOULD NOT set (round 2, I-1).
- An admitted commit reload in the config fence waits up to its apply budget (`capBudget`, at most 600 s), so a stop that meets one can exceed `TimeoutStopSec` or gokrazy's 100 s and be killed; the drain and the teardown stay bounded (round 2, I-8).
- Without bgp-rib, routes already advertised are not re-advertised with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN; the drain logs the refusal (AC-26).
- One duplicate UPDATE per key is accepted for a write made inside the window that runs from the scope store until the cut M is read (redesign round 1, N-2): a write after the store loads the new scope, and one made before its destination's take-and-release, or after that release and before M is read, goes out drained with a message ID at or below M, so the walk replays it once more in the same drained form (RFC 4271 Section 9.2 is a SHOULD NOT; the duplicate changes nothing at the peer). Closing it would need the cut per destination, read inside each take-and-release, which the reactor's one message-ID counter does not give.
- Maintenance drain without stop, and its cancel: its own spec, `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` (Q-2; owner, 2026-10-13: "we want runtime but it need design").
- RFC 8326 Section 4.3 per-neighbour knob ("configuration knobs that utilize the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community to inform BGP neighbors in preparation for an impending neighbor shutdown"): this spec gives the whole-router stop knob only; a knob for one neighbour's maintenance is a gap, designed with the runtime drain in `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` (round 3, I-7).
- An external plugin that re-announces learned paths with `update text` is classed as an origination at the writer, by construction: its write carries an `originated` owner with no receipt and no peer-up provenance, so Ze cannot tell it from a local origination, and it is tagged as originated (round 3, I-1).
- A drain whose `DrainEventsApplied` returns an error (deadline, a retained earlier delivery failure, or a stopped owner) skips the walk with a line carrying that error (round 4, N-5), and a walk that fails is logged the same way; the stop then proceeds to the wait and the teardown as it does today, and the "drain finished" line says the replay was not completed (AC-36). Paths already advertised then keep their attributes, and paths written during the drain are still tagged (B-2). A retained delivery failure lasts for bgp-rib's process lifetime ("Success never clears it", `process/delivery.go`), so it fails every later walk of that process too.
- A walk retried after a failed walk (the runtime spec's Q-9 (a) operator retry; the stop drain walks once) runs at the same epoch, which bgp-rib accepts, and reads a new cut M, so every live drained write made since the failed walk's cut is replayed once more in the same drained form: one duplicate UPDATE per such key per retry, beside the scope-store duplicate above (redesign round 2, N-2).

## RFC Documentation (Scope: protocol)

Add `// RFC NNNN Section X.Y: "<quoted requirement>"` above enforcing code: RFC 8326 Section 4.2 (outbound bullet, inbound bullet, whole-router tagging) and Section 4 (LOCAL_PREF 0) above the drain verdict's tag branches, and Section 4.2's scope at the reflected branch, with Section 1 ("Other maintenance cases, such as the shutdown of an IBGP session ... are out of scope for this document.") and Appendix C.1 ("no specific graceful shutdown action is required", informative) quoted there (round 3, N-5); RFC 8326 Section 4.3 at the `drain-on-shutdown` leaf's handler; RFC 4456 Section 10 only at the branch that keeps a reflected path's LOCAL_PREF; RFC 7947 Section 2.2.4 at the route-server-client branch; RFC 4271 Section 9.2 at the writer's withholding of an untouched drain-replay path (round 2, I-3); RFC 6987 Section 2 above the MaxLinkMetric origination (v2 and v3); RFC 3787 above the overload set; RFC 4724 Section 5 at BGP's restart-kind answer; RFC 3623 Section 2.1 ("If Router X wants to ensure that its neighbors receive the grace-LSAs, it should retransmit the grace-LSAs until they are acknowledged (i.e., perform standard OSPF reliable flooding of the grace-LSAs).") above OSPF's wait for the Grace-LSA acknowledgements, and RFC 3623 Appendix A ("Encodes the reason for the router restart as one of the following: 0 (unknown), 1 (software restart), 2 (software reload/upgrade) or 3 (switch to redundant control processor).") with Section 3.1 policy (c) ("c) only help on software reloads/upgrades") above the mapping from restart cause to grace reason (round 4, I-7, I-8). For the injected-prefix drain (Q-8): RFC 2328 Section 16.4 step (1) ("If the cost specified by the LSA is LSInfinity, or if the LSA's LS age is equal to MaxAge, then examine the next LSA.") and Appendix B, LSInfinity, above the OSPF drain external metric; RFC 5305 Section 4 and RFC 5308 Section 2 (the MAX_PATH_METRIC and MAX_V6_PATH_METRIC sentences quoted in Data Flow) above IS-IS's wide drain metric; RFC 1195 Section 3.2 above the narrow 63. Raising OSPF stub links and OSPFv3 stub prefixes is the owner's Q-8 choice and not an RFC 6987 obligation: RFC 6987 Section 2 raises non-stub links only, so its comment stays on the MaxLinkMetric origination, `always` keeps the stub links' cost, and the stub raise carries a comment naming Q-8 and the reason (stub networks reached through Ze must move too) rather than an RFC quote.

RFC 8326 ledger (Q-4 (a), owner 2026-10-13: "a" (lift the RFC freeze for the RFC 8326 sender side; ledger updated, extraction walk owed)). The 2026-10-08 RFC freeze is lifted for the sender side of RFC 8326 only, and this spec updates the ledger: `rfc/short/rfc8326.md` moves from `Enrolment: blocked` and `Support: -`, its requirement rows cover the sender obligations in the mapping below, each row a verbatim span of the section it cites (`./le rfc check`), with the Section 4 SHOULD recorded as an owner-approved deviation (Q-10) and the Section 4.3 knob as partial. The extraction walk is owed by this spec: the walk of `rfc/full/rfc8326.txt`, both arithmetics (forward: every normative site mapped or excluded with a reason; reverse: every declared requirement the target of a site or declared `unsourced-ids`), signed off in `rfc/extraction/rfc8326.json` as `docs/contributing/rfc-conformance-gates.md` describes, and no RFC 8326 conformance number is published before that sign-off. The receiver side (Section 4.1, inbound LOCAL_PREF on received GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN) stays outside this spec and is recorded in the summary as an implementation gap. Every tag this spec adds carries its discrimination record (`./le rfc discriminate-record`).

RFC 8326 Section 4.2 mapping (I-11):

| Section 4.2 step | Ze mechanism | Status |
|------------------|--------------|--------|
| outbound policy tags the paths propagated over the session with GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN | drain verdict, every path to an eBGP destination, except learned paths to a route-server client (RFC 7947 Section 2.2.4) | implemented by this spec |
| inbound policy tags the paths received over the session and sets a low LOCAL_PREF | substituted procedure: egress LOCAL_PREF 0 and the tag on the iBGP copies of eBGP-received paths; Ze's own Loc-RIB unchanged | a procedure step, not an obligation (no RFC 2119 keyword), so nothing is recorded against it; the ledger row is the Section 4 SHOULD below (Q-10, round 2 I-2) |
| Section 4: "The LOCAL_PREF value SHOULD be lower than any of the alternative paths." | LOCAL_PREF 0 on every tagged iBGP copy | met at every iBGP receiver; residue: Ze's own Loc-RIB not lowered. Under Q-10 (b), recorded in `rfc/short/rfc8326.md` as an owner-approved deviation of this SHOULD for the initiator's own selection, Section 4 cited, not counted conformant |
| waits for re-advertisement and convergence | the configured `drain-on-shutdown` wait, bounded by the deadline | implemented |
| shuts down the EBGP session, optionally using RFC 8203 | Cease subcode 2 Administrative Shutdown after the drain | implemented (unchanged) |
| whole router: tag originated routes and set LOCAL_PREF low | drain verdict and f at `Session.writeUpdateBody` (origination provenance), including toward route-server clients; LOCAL_PREF 0 on iBGP copies (LOCAL_PREF is not sent on eBGP) | implemented by this spec |
| Section 4.3: "BGP Implementers SHOULD provide configuration knobs that utilize the GRACEFUL_SHUTDOWN community to inform BGP neighbors in preparation for an impending neighbor shutdown." | `bgp drain-on-shutdown <seconds>` (Q-6), a knob for the whole-router stop | partial: met for the whole-router stop; the per-neighbour maintenance knob is a gap, designed with the runtime drain in `plan/spec-runtime-drain-and-undrain.md` (Q-2) and recorded in `rfc/short/rfc8326.md` under Q-4 (a) (round 3, I-7) |

## Checklist

### Pre-Spec Verification (before the design is presented)
- [ ] Metadata table present, with a valid Status, Depends, Phase and Updated
- [ ] `ai/INDEX.md` keyword table checked
- [ ] An `rfc/short/` summary exists for every RFC referenced
- [ ] Template format followed: the 🧪 emoji, tables rather than prose, `[ ]` never `[x]`
- [ ] No code snippets
- [ ] Files to Modify names feature code, not only tests
- [ ] Current Behavior and Data Flow sections completed
- [ ] AC-N rows carry testable assertions
- [ ] Every assumption has a Basis and a validation method; every failure mode is a risk row
- [ ] Required Reading carries `→ Decision:` / `→ Constraint:` checkpoints
- [ ] Integration Checklist marks "CLI grammar" when a command is added, "Doctor check" when a runtime dependency is

### Goal Gates (MUST pass)
- [ ] AC-1..AC-N all demonstrated
- [ ] Every user story has a working path and a passing test
- [ ] Wiring Test table complete: every row a concrete test name, none deferred
- [ ] `./le verify worktree` passes
- [ ] Feature code integrated (`internal/*`, `cmd/*`), not library-only
- [ ] Integration and Documentation checklists answered Yes/No/N-A with evidence
- [ ] Architectural Verification table filled, including registration over hardcoding
- [ ] Critical Review passes, and `ai/rules/quality.md` is satisfied
- [ ] Every A-N confirmed or broken, none `unvalidated`
- [ ] Every item this spec did not do is a spec of its own, named here, in its own bucket

### TDD
- [ ] Tests written
- [ ] Tests FAIL (paste output)
- [ ] Tests PASS (paste output)
- [ ] Boundary tests for all numeric inputs
- [ ] Functional `.ci` tests for end-to-end behavior
- [ ] Interop tests for protocol features

### Closure
- [ ] Append `plan/TEMPLATE-CLOSURE.md` and complete every section in it
- [ ] `/ze-review` gate clean, recorded via `internal/le/spec/review.go`
- [ ] Any lesson routed to its governing surface under `ai/rules/planning.md`
- [ ] **Commit A:** code + tests + docs + edited spec + any journal rows owed by the work
- [ ] **Commit B:** `remove plan/immediate/spec-daemon-drain-before-stop.md` only, in the same `./le commit create` script
